# Domain Docs

How the engineering skills should consume this repo's domain documentation when exploring the codebase.

## Before exploring, read these

- **`GLOSSARY.md`** at the repo root, or
- **`GLOSSARY-MAP.md`** at the repo root if it exists: it points at one `GLOSSARY.md` per context. Read each one relevant to the topic.
- **`docs/adr/`**: read ADRs that touch the area you're about to work in. In multi-context repos, also check `src/<context>/docs/adr/` for context-scoped decisions.

If any of these files don't exist, **proceed silently**. Don't flag their absence; don't suggest creating them upfront. The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.

## File structure

Single-context repo (most repos):

```
/
├── GLOSSARY.md
├── docs/adr/
│   ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md
│   └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md
└── src/
```

Multi-context repo (presence of `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` at the root):

```
/
├── GLOSSARY-MAP.md
├── docs/adr/                          ← system-wide decisions
└── src/
    ├── ordering/
    │   ├── GLOSSARY.md
    │   └── docs/adr/                  ← context-specific decisions
    └── billing/
        ├── GLOSSARY.md
        └── docs/adr/
```

## Use the glossary's vocabulary

When your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `GLOSSARY.md`. Don't drift to synonyms the glossary explicitly avoids.

If the concept you need isn't in the glossary yet, that's a signal: either you're inventing language the project doesn't use (reconsider) or there's a real gap (note it for `/domain-modeling`).

## Flag ADR conflicts

If your output contradicts an existing ADR, surface it explicitly rather than silently overriding:

> _Contradicts ADR-0007 (event-sourced orders), but worth reopening because…_

# 01: Walking skeleton: one incident, mock LLM, report on disk

**What to build:** A runnable end-to-end path through the whole system with the thinnest possible content. A developer runs the investigate CLI on one small incident with a mock LLM backend. A minimal LangGraph graph calls the logs tool once and writes a schema-valid InvestigationReport. This fixes the project layout, the core Pydantic schemas (Scenario, Evidence, ToolResult, Finding, Hypothesis, InvestigationReport; see the PRD's data model sketch), and the test harness that every later ticket builds on.

**Blocked by:** None (can start immediately)

**Status:** ready-for-agent

Context: `incident-investigator-ai-ready-prd.md` (data model sketch, step 8), `DECISIONS.md` Q1, Q4, Q22.

- [ ] One small incident (inc01) and its answer key are stored separately. The answer key is never loaded by agent code

# Multi-Agent Production Incident Investigator

Sources: `orange_invest_assignment.md` (the brief) and `DECISIONS.md` (Q1–Q24, the decision record).
This PRD adds the interview of 2026-11-12 (L24–L26) and recommendations still awaiting sign-off (D#).

## TL;DR

A Python CLI and API that investigates simulated production incidents with six cooperating agents. The agents share a typed blackboard and a task DAG, and a Critic can send the investigation back for more evidence. The system returns ranked, evidence-cited hypotheses, or an honest `INCONCLUSIVE`.

The thesis is to reuse LangGraph for the control flow and the Claude Code CLI for the LLM, and to build only the investigation logic, the simulated data, and a fair evaluation against a single-agent baseline.

The audience is the reviewer of the AI Engineer assignment. The budget is one overnight build (Q3).

## Locked assumptions

- **Language/runtime:** Python 3.12, managed with `uv` (Q1).
- **Framework:** LangGraph (Q14). FastAPI for the API (Q19). Pydantic v2 for all schemas. *Exact package versions are pinned in `uv.lock` at scaffold time (D9).*
- **LLM access:** Claude through the user's subscription via `claude -p --output-format json` (Q15). There is no API key today (Q13).
- **Models:** Opus 5.5 for plan creation, Reasoning, Critic, the baseline and the judge. Haiku 5.5 for Observability, Dependency and Reporting (Q8).
- **Deployment:** local only, on the developer's macOS machine. No hosting, no database, no remote git (Q19, Q22).
- **Data:** 10 hand-written synthetic in

# Design Decisions — Multi-Agent Incident Investigation System

Source brief: `orange_invest_assignment.md`

How to use this file: each question has a recommended answer. Write your answer on the `Answer:` line.
"yes" accepts the recommendation. Settled answers stay here as the decision record and later feed the 500-word technical note.

---

## Round 1

### Q1 — Language
Should the system be written in Python?
The alternatives are TypeScript or another language. Python has the most mature agent libraries and makes evaluation and plotting easy. A Python 3.12 venv already exists here.

**Recommended:** Yes, Python 3.12, managed with `uv`.

Answer: **Yes.** Python 3.12 with `uv`.

---

### Q2 — LLM provider
Should we use Anthropic Claude?
One option is a strong model for the Reasoning and Critic agents and a cheaper one for Observability, Dependency and Reporting. That split also gives the token and latency comparison something interesting to show.
The alternatives are OpenAI, Gemini, or a local model through Ollama, which is free but weaker and slower.

**Recommended:** Yes, Claude: Sonnet 5.5 for Reasoning and Critic, Haiku 5.5 for the other agents. The baseline uses Sonnet.

Answer: **Yes, Claude.** Use Opus 5.5 where it is needed. Which agents get which model is decided in round 2 (Q8).

---

### Q3 — Time budget and depth
Is this a 3–5 day submission where depth beats breadth?
That means all 6 agents, 10–12 incidents and a solid evaluation, with only a CLI and no extras like a UI or a vector database for past incidents.
Or is there a hard deadline or a smaller budget that should shrink the scope?

**Recommended:** Yes, about 3–5 days with a CLI only. Past incidents are found by keyword or tag search, not embeddings.

Answer: **No, the budget is smaller.** Finish today, overnight at most. v1 is a CLI only, with no UI. A FastAPI layer with Swagger may come later if needed.

---

### Q4 — How agents collaborate
Should they use a shared "blackboard"?
Every agent reads from and writes typed findings to one shared investigation state. The Orchestrator runs a list of tasks with dependencies between them.
The Critic can add follow-up tasks, such as "check metrics for svc-B between 10:00 and 10:15", which sends the investigation back through the loop.
The alternative is free-form messages between agents, which is harder to bound and test.
The brief says simply chaining calls isn't enough, and this model directly shows shared state, task dependencies and feedback loops.

**Recommended:** Yes, a blackboard with typed Pydantic findings, a task DAG, and a Critic that can add tasks.

Answer: **Yes.** Blackboard, task DAG, and a Critic that can add tasks.

---

### Q5 — Simulated data
Should each test incident be a hand-written JSON scenario?
Each scenario contains the logs, metrics, deploys, dependency graph and past incidents. The answer key sits in a separate file the agents never see.
Each scenario also sets which tools fail, how (a timeout or a partial 
… [truncated 15085 chars]

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Stop hook: export the current Claude Code session transcript into docs/transcript/.

Writes <session_id>.jsonl (raw copy) and <session_id>.md (readable) on every turn,
so the full history survives context compaction.
"""

import json
import os
import shutil
import sys
from pathlib import Path

MAX_TOOL_CHARS = 3000


def clip(text: str) -> str:
    return text if len(text) <= MAX_TOOL_CHARS else text[:MAX_TOOL_CHARS] + f"\n… [truncated {len(text) - MAX_TOOL_CHARS} chars]"


def render_block(block) -> str:
    if isinstance(block, str):
        return block
    kind = block.get("type")
    if kind == "text":
        return block.get("text", "")
    if kind == "tool_use":
        args = json.dumps(block.get("input", {}), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
        return f"**Tool call — `{block.get('name')}`**\n```json\n{clip(args)}\n```"
    if kind == "tool_result":
accelerate
accelerate-config
accelerate-estimate-memory
accelerate-launch
accelerate-merge-weights
activate
activate_this.py
activate.bat

{
  "file_path": "/Users/<user>/Documents/Learning/Orange/src/incident_investigator/dataset.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Loads incident scenarios and, for evaluation only, their answer keys.\n\nThis is the only module that knows where answer keys live. Agent, graph and\ntool code must never import `load_answer_key` (tests/test_answer_key_isolation.py).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom incident_investigator.schemas import AnswerKey, Scenario\n\nDATA_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2] / \"data\"\n_INCIDENT_ID = re.compile(r\"^inc\\d{2}$\")\n\n\nclass UnknownIncidentError(LookupError):\n    pass\n\n\ndef list_incident_ids(data_dir: Path = DATA_DIR) -> list[str]:\n    return sorted(p.stem for p in (data_dir / \"incidents\").glob(\"inc*.json\"))\n\n\ndef _path(folder: str, incident_id: str, data_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    path = data_dir / folder / f\"{incident_id}.json\"\n    if not _INCIDENT_ID.match(incident_id) or not path.is_file():\n        known = \", \".join(list_incident_ids(data_dir)) or \"none\"\n        raise UnknownIncidentError(f\"unknown incident {incident_id!r} (known: {known})\")\n    return path\n\n\ndef load_scenario(incident_id: str, data_dir: Path = DATA_DIR) -> Scenario:\n    return Scenario.model_validate_json(_path(\"incidents\", incident_id, data_dir).read_text())\n\n\ndef load_answer_key(incident_id: str, data_dir: Path = DATA_DIR) -> AnswerKey:\n    return AnswerKey.model_validate_json(_path(\"answer_keys\", incident_id, data_dir).read_text())\n"
}

  "content": "import re\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\n\nfrom incident_investigator.dataset import load_scenario\nfrom incident_investigator.tools.logs import LogsQuery, query_logs\n\n\ndef _utc(hh: int, mm: int) -> datetime:\n    return datetime(2026, 11, 10, hh, mm, tzinfo=timezone.utc)\n\n\ndef test_logs_for_a_service_in_a_window_return_stable_evidence_ids():\n    scenario = load_scenario(\"inc01\")\n\n    result = query_logs(\n        scenario, LogsQuery(service=\"checkout-svc\", start=_utc(10, 0), end=_utc(10, 30))\n    )\n\n    assert result.status == \"ok\"\n    assert [e.id for e in result.evidence] == [\n        \"log:inc01:0002\",\n        \"log:inc01:0003\",\n        \"log:inc01:0004\",\n        \"log:inc01:0006\",\n    ]\n    assert all(re.fullmatch(r\"log:inc01:\\d{4}\", e.id) for e in result.evidence)\n    assert all(e.kind == \"log\" for e in result.evidence)\n    assert result.evidence[1].payload[\"message\"].startswith(\"NullPointerException\")\n\n\ndef test_level_filter_keeps_only_matching_rows():\n    scenario = load_scenario(\"inc01\")\n\n    result = query_logs(\n        scenario,\n        LogsQuery(service=\"checkout-svc\", start=_utc(9, 0), end=_utc(12, 0), level=\"ERROR\"),\n    )\n\n    assert [e.id for e in result.evidence] == [\"log:inc01:0003\", \"log:inc01:0004\", \"log:inc01:0006\"]\n\n\ndef test_same_query_returns_same_ids_every_time():\n    scenario = load_scenario(\"inc01\")\n    query = LogsQuery(service=\"checkout-svc\", start=_utc(9, 0), end=_utc(12, 0))\n\n    assert query_logs(scenario, query) == query_logs(scenario, query)\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/<user>/Documents/Learning/Orange/src/incident_investigator/tools/logs.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Logs tool: log rows for one service in a time window, as Evidence.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom typing import Literal\n\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict\n\nfrom incident_investigator.schemas import Evidence, Scenario, ToolResult\n\n\nclass LogsQuery(BaseModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"forbid\")\n\n    service: str\n    start: datetime\n    end: datetime\n    level: Literal[\"DEBUG\", \"INFO\", \"WARN\", \"ERROR\"] | None = None\n\n\ndef query_logs(scenario: Scenario, query: LogsQuery) -> ToolResult:\n    rows = [\n        row\n        for row in scenario.logs\n        if row.service == query.service\n        and query.start <= row.timestamp <= query.end\n        and (query.level is None or row.level == query.level)\n    ]\n    evidence = [\n        Evidence(\n            id=row.id,\n            kind=\"log\",\n            timestamp=row.timestamp,\n            payload={\"service\": row.service, \"level\": row.level, \"message\": row.message},\n        )\n        for row in rows\n    ]\n    return ToolResult(status=\"ok\", evidence=evidence)\n"
}
```

at least one hypothesis\"\n\n\ndef test_report_only_cites_evidence_that_exists_in_the_scenario(tmp_path: Path):\n    main([\"inc01\", \"--llm\", \"mock\", \"--out\", str(tmp_path)])\n    report =   "file_path": "/Users/<user>/Documents/Learning/Orange/src/incident_investigator/llm/mock.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"A deterministic stand-in LLM for tests and offline runs.\n\nIt knows nothing about any incident. It answers every prompt with a `finish`\naction built only from what the prompt shows it: the affected service and the\nevidence IDs it was given.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport re\n\nfrom incident_investigator.llm.base import LLMResponse, Usage\n\n_EVIDENCE_ID = re.compile(r'\"id\":\\s*\"([a-z]+:inc\\d{2}:[^\"]+)\"')\n_SERVICE = re.compile(r'\"affected_service\":\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"')\n\n\nclass MockLLM:\n    def complete(self, *, system: str, prompt: str, model: str) -> LLMResponse:\n        ids = list(dict.fromkeys(_EVIDENCE_ID.findall(prompt)))\n        match = _SERVICE.search(prompt)\n        service = match.group(1) if match else \"unknown\"\n        facts = [{\"statement\": f\"{len(ids)} log rows returned for {service}\", \"evidence_ids\": ids[:3]}]\n        action = {\n            \"action\": \"finish\",\n            \"facts\": facts if ids else [],\n            \"assumptions\": [\n                {\n                    \"statement\": f\"The errors in {service} are caused by a recent change\",\n                    \"basis\": \"Mock reasoning: errors appear inside the incident window\",\n                    \"how_to_verify\": \"Check deploy history for the service\",\n                }\n            ],\n            \"hypotheses\": [\n                {\n                    \"id\": \"h1\",\n                    \"service\": service,\n                    \"category\": \"bad_deploy\",\n                    \"summary\": f\"A recent deploy broke {service}\",\n                    \"supporting_ids\": ids[:3],\n                    \"proposed_confidence\": 0.7 if ids else 0.3,\n                }\n            ],\n        }\n        return LLMResponse(text=json.dumps(action), usage=Usage(tokens_in=len(prompt) // 4, tokens_out=50))\n"
}

  "content": "\"\"\"The single `investigate()` entry point shared by the CLI and (later) the API.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport time\n\nfrom incident_investigator.dataset import load_scenario\nfrom incident_investigator.graph import Blackboard, build_graph\nfrom incident_investigator.llm.base import LLMClient\nfrom incident_investigator.schemas import InvestigationReport\n\n\ndef investigate(incident_id: str, llm: LLMClient) -> InvestigationReport:\n    \"\"\"Run one investigation.   "content": "\"\"\"`investigate <incident_id>`: run one investigation and write the report as JSON.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom incident_investigator.dataset import UnknownIncidentError\nfrom incident_investigator.investigate import investigate\nfrom incident_investigator.llm.base import LLMClient\nfrom incident_investigator.llm.mock import MockLLM\n\n\ndef _llm(name: str) -> LLMClient:\n    if name == \"mock\":\n        return MockLLM()\n    raise ValueError(f\"unknown LLM backend {name!r}\")\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"investigate\", description=__doc__)\n    parser.add_argument(\"incident_id\", help=\"incident to investigate, e.g. inc01\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--mode\", choices=[\"multi\"], default=\"multi\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--llm\", choices=[\"mock\"], default=\"mock\", help=\"LLM backend\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--out\", type=Path, default=Path(\"runs\"), help=\"report output folder\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n\n    try:\n        report = investigate(args.incident_id, _llm(args.llm))\n    except UnknownIncidentError as exc:\n        print(f\"error: {exc}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        return 2\n\n    args.out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    path = args.out / f\"{report.incident_id}__{args.mode}.json\"\n    path.write_text(report.model_dump_json(indent=2))\n    print(f\"{report.outcome}: report written to {path}\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n"

Each is a judgement call, never a hard violation, and a documented repo standard overrides it:\n- Mysterious Name: name doesn't reveal what it does/holds → rename.\n- Duplicated Code: same logic shape in more than one hunk/file → extract.\n- Feature Envy: method reaches into another object's data more than its own → move it.\n- Data Clumps: same few fields/params travel together → bundle into a type.\n- Primitive Obsession: primitive/string standing in for a domain concept → own small type.\n- Repeated Switches: same switch/if-cascade on same type recurs → polymorphism or shared map.\n- Shotgun Surgery: one logical change forces scattered edits → gather into one module.\n- Divergent Change: one module edited for several unrelated reasons → split.\n- Speculative Generality: abstraction/params/hooks for needs the spec doesn't have → delete/inline.\n- Message Chains: long a.b().c().d() navigation → hide behind one method.\n- Middle Man: class/function that mostly delegates → cut it.\n- Refused Bequest: subclass/implementer ignoring most of what it inherits → composition.\n\nContext: this is a deliberately thin walking skeleton; later tickets (.scratch/incident-investigator/issues/02-13) add the real LLM backend, more tools, the full agent DAG, evidence rules, etc. Don't flag the absence of later-ticket features.\n\nBrief: Report, per file/hunk where relevant, (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. ### Assistant · 2026-11-12T15:30:07.398Z

Both reviews are back. Here they are as the review skill asks, followed by what I fixed.

## Standards
No hard breaches. The repo's documented standards cover only the issue-tracker process, and the diff follows them. All of these are judgement calls:
- **Glossary drift:** `Scenario.report` (the incident) and `Blackboard.report` (the investigation report) share a name.
- **Data clump / duplication:** `tokens_in/out/cache_tokens` is defined in `Usage`, `Counters` and `ReportMetrics`, and the graph adds them field by field. A `Counters.add(usage)` method would fix it.
- **Primitive obsession:** the outcome is spelled two ways (`root_cause` vs `ROOT_CAUSE`), log levels are repeated in two models, and the `Finding(task_id="t1", agent="observability")` call uses magic strings.
- **Shotgun surgery risk:** the evidence kinds are listed both in `Evidence.kind` and in `Scenario._rows`.
- **Mysterious names:** `Confidence` is a `Field`, not a type, and the initial-state variable in `investigate.py` is called `start`.
- **Hidden coupling:** `MockLLM` finds what it needs by running regexes over the graph's prompt.
- **Minor speculative generality:** the `--mode` flag and the `_llm()` factory each have only one option for now.

## Spec
Every checkbox was checked independently: 18 tests pass, `inc01` exits 0 with a valid report, and `inc99` exits 2 and writes no report. Findings:
- **Partial:** the validator only checks the `log:inc01:` prefix, so an ID like `log:inc01:x` would pass.
- **Wrong:** a timezone-naive `LogsQuery` raises `TypeError`. That conflicts with ticket 03's rule that bad input returns an error instead of raising.
- **Wrong:** the mock hard-codes `bad_deploy`, which happens to be inc01's answer, and it cites the first 3 IDs it sees, including a `GET /health 200` row.
- **Design conflict:** the Blackboard lives in `graph.py` and holds the whole `Scenario`, so agents could read data without going through the tools (Q4). Tickets 04, 06 and 11 will need to reshape it.
- **Gaps:** `dependencies` has no evidence IDs, which ticket 03 needs. The isolation test only looks for the strings `answer_key`/`AnswerKey`, so reading the file through a path built another way would not be caught.

I'm fixing the two cheap correctness items now, test first: the full evidence-ID pattern and requiring timezone-aware datetimes.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T15:30:38.194Z

Ticket 01 (walking skeleton) is done and committed to a new local git repo with no remote. All 20 tests pass and the typecheck is clean.

**What works now:**
- `uv run investigate inc01` runs one investigation with the mock LLM and writes `runs/inc01__multi.json`, a valid `InvestigationReport` (exit 0).
- `uv run investigate inc99` prints `error: unknown incident 'inc99' (known: inc01)`, exits 2 and writes no report.
- The project uses Python 3.12 with uv, and the lockfile pins LangGraph 1.2.20, Pydantic 2.14.3 and pytest 9.2.0.
- The core data models from the PRD are in `src/incident_investigator/schemas.py`. Scenario data checks that every evidence ID has the right form and is unique.
- inc01 is a bad deploy with a CPU-spike trap. Its answer key is stored in a separate folder. Only `dataset.py` knows about answer keys, and a test fails if any other source file mentions them.
- The logs tool returns evidence with IDs like `log:inc01:0003`.
- The investigation runs as a small LangGraph graph: one logs-tool call, one LLM call, then the report.

**Tests:** they sit at the four points you approved: data loading and schemas, the logs tool, the CLI end to end, and the answer-key guard. Each was written to fail first, then passed. While adding the guard it also flagged `schemas.py`, because the `AnswerKey` model was defined there, so I moved that model into `dataset.py`.

**Review:** the coding-standards review found no rule breaches, only design smells. The spec review found two bugs, which I fixed with tests first:
- The evidence-ID check accepted IDs like `log:inc01:x`. It now enforces the full pattern.
- A time window without a timezone crashed the logs tool. Times must now carry a timezone.

Several findings belong to later tickets, so I recorded them in the Comments section of the 01 ticket file. These matter most:
- **Ticket 04:** the shared investigation state holds the whole scenario. That lets agents read data without calling a tool, which goes against Q4, so it needs reshaping.
- **Tickets 08/09:** the mock LLM always proposes `bad_deploy`, which is inc01's real answer, and it cites a harmless `GET /health 200` log. Mock runs therefore can't be used for accuracy or grounding scores.

**Commits:**
- `fe49e36` the existing brief, decisions, PRD and tickets
- `dafa245` ticket 01
- `c132cbd` the two review fixes

**One thing to watch:** `.venv` already held your docling install, so I installed with `uv sync --inexact` to keep it. A plain `uv sync` would remove docling. `uv run` leaves it alone.

Tickets 02 and 03 are now unblocked.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T15:38:31.944Z

Ticket 03 is merged into `main`, and the full suite passes (53 tests, pyright clean). Ticket 02 (Claude CLI backend) and ticket 08 (the 10-incident dataset) are now running in parallel.

Three things from 03 matter for the next tickets:
- **Failure counts are per run.** Each tool counts its calls inside a `ToolSession`, so ticket 04 must create one session per investigation.
- **Gaps need copying.** Each failed call returns an `EvidenceGap` with its result, and ticket 04 has to put it on the shared blackboard.
- **Call order must be stable.** If parallel agents share one session, their calls have to happen in the same order every run, or "the Nth call fails" will hit a different call each time. I'll put this in the 04 brief.

`Scenario.dependencies` is now a list of rows with evidence IDs instead of a dict, which differs from the PRD sketch. That was needed so dependency evidence can be cited.

Once 02 finishes, I'll merge it and start 04 and 12.

# Which model each role uses (DECISIONS.md Q8; Q20 allows a local model per role).
# Opus for the hard thinking, Haiku for agents that mostly call tools and summarise.

[roles]
planner = "claude-opus-5-5"
reasoning = "claude-opus-5-5"
critic = "claude-opus-5-5"
baseline = "claude-opus-5-5"
judge = "claude-opus-5-5"
observability = "claude-haiku-5-5"
dependency = "claude-haiku-5-5"
reporting = "claude-haiku-5-5"
"""Load the per-role model choice from models.toml (stdlib tomllib, no YAML dependency)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import tomllib
from pathlib import Path

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, ValidationError

DEFAULT_PATH = Path(__file__).with_name("models.toml")


class ModelsConfigError(ValueError):
    pass


class _Roles(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")

    planner: str
    reasoning: str
    critic: str
    baseline: str
    judge: str
    observability: str
    dependency: str
    reporting: str


class ModelsConfig(BaseModel):
    roles: _Roles

    def model_for(self, role: str) -> str:
        if role not in _Roles.model_fields:
            raise KeyError(f"unknown role {role!r}")
        return getattr(self.roles, role)


def load_models(path: Path | None = None) -> ModelsConfig:
    source = path or DEFAULT_PATH
    try:
        return ModelsConfig.model_validate(tomllib.loads(source.read_text()))
    except (ValidationError, tomllib.TOMLDecodeError) as exc:
        raise ModelsConfigError(f"invalid models config {source}: {exc}") from exc

"""Post-processing between the investigation and the report (seam for ticket 05).

Ticket 05 replaces the body of `apply_evidence_rules` with the deterministic rules
(drop or demote Facts citing unknown IDs, cap unsupported hypotheses at 0.5, lower
confidence by 0.1 per open gap). It reads only the blackboard, so the single-agent
baseline (ticket 07) can call the same function.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from incident_investigator.blackboard import Blackboard
from incident_investigator.schemas import Hypothesis


def apply_evidence_rules(board: Blackboard) -> list[Hypothesis]:
    """Return the hypotheses with `final_confidence` set. Today: final = proposed."""
    return [h.model_copy(update={"final_confidence": h.proposed_confidence}) for h in board.hypotheses]


def rank(hypotheses: list[Hypothesis]) -> list[Hypothesis]:
    """Highest final confidence first; ties keep the Reasoning agent's order."""
    return sorted(hypotheses, key=lambda h: h.final_confidence or 0.0, reverse=True)
```

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T15:57:36.218Z

Ticket 04 is merged. `main` now has tickets 01, 02, 03, 04, 08 and 12, with 223 tests passing and pyright clean.

**What 04 added:**
- **Planner:** one planning call to the model turns the incident into a list of tasks with dependencies.
- **Task runner:** plain Python runs tasks with no dependency between them in parallel. Agents share a typed blackboard that holds the incident report but never the scenario, so they only learn about the incident by calling tools.
- **Repeatable failures:** tools are called in a fixed order across parallel tasks, so a scheduled tool failure always lands on the same task.
- **Per-role models:** the graph now gives each role its own backend, which finishes ticket 12's loose end.
- **Trace file:** the CLI now also saves the full blackboard as `<id>__multi.trace.json`.
- **Live run on inc01:** it found the right answer (bad deploy on checkout-svc, 0.8) ahead of three competing hypotheses. It took 77 s, with 15 LLM calls and 8 tool calls.

**Departures from the ticket:**
- Reporting is a fixed final step rather than a planned task. Code ranks the hypotheses, and the model only writes the recommended actions.
- The ticket asked for supporting and contradicting evidence on each hypothesis, but code only checks that there are at least two with supporting evidence. Contradicting evidence isn't required, so the model isn't pushed to invent it. In the live run the top hypothesis had none.

Tickets 05 (evidence rules and INCONCLUSIVE outcome) and 06 (Critic loop and run limits) are running now. Ticket 07 (single-agent baseline) starts once both are merged.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T16:05:40.054Z

Ticket 05 is merged. `main` now has tickets 01–05, 08 and 12, with 241 tests passing and pyright clean.

**How the evidence rules work (all plain code, never the model):**
- **What counts as real evidence:** an evidence ID counts only if a tool actually returned it during this run.
- **Bad citations:** a Fact that cites an unknown ID becomes an Assumption that names the bad IDs. It is never dropped silently. Unknown IDs on a hypothesis, and actions that point to no hypothesis, are removed.
- **Confidence:** a hypothesis with no supporting Fact is capped at 0.5. Each evidence gap that affects it takes off 0.1. When a gap doesn't name the hypotheses it affects, a fixed table maps each tool to the root-cause categories it covers.
- **Outcome:** if no hypothesis reaches 0.6, the outcome is INCONCLUSIVE, and the report still lists the ranked hypotheses and the gaps.
- **Audit log:** every change the rules make is recorded on the report as a `rule_adjustment`.

**Limits to know about:**
- **inc09 with the mock:** the offline mock reaches ROOT_CAUSE at exactly 0.6 on inc09, which is meant to be inconclusive. So the end-to-end INCONCLUSIVE test uses a scripted run on inc01 instead. This affects only mock runs, but it is another reason mock runs say nothing about accuracy.
- **Gaps don't record the service:** a gap doesn't record which service the failed query was about, so it lowers hypotheses about any service.
- **Gaps never close:** every gap counts as open for the whole run.

I've told the 06 agent (Critic loop and run limits, still running) to pull in 05 and where the small conflict will be. Ticket 07 (single-agent baseline) starts once 06 merges.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T16:10:08.496Z

Ticket 06 is merged. `main` now has tickets 01–06, 08 and 12, with 255 tests passing and pyright clean.

**What 06 added:**
- **Critic loop:** a Critic agent reviews the hypotheses and can add follow-up tasks. These go back through the scheduler, for at most 2 extra loops.
- **Run limits:** the run stops at 30 tool calls, 300k tokens or 10 minutes. Each limit sets its own `terminated_by` value, and a report is still written from whatever the blackboard holds at that point.
- **Agent failures:** an agent that fails twice is recorded on the report's new `failures` field, and the run carries on without it.
- **Shared limits:** all the limit values live in one `limits.py`, so the baseline can use the same object.

**Departures from the ticket:**
- **Third Critic call:** the Critic is asked once more after loop 2, so the run can tell `critic_limit` (it still wanted more) apart from `done`.
- **Reporting after a hard stop:** when the token or time limit stops the run, the report has no recommended actions, because the model call that writes them is skipped.
- **Task stopped by a limit:** it is marked `failed`, but no AgentFailure is recorded.
- **Wall clock:** the 10-minute limit can't stop a model call that's already running. Each call is still capped at 120 s, so a run can go about 2 minutes over.

**Test speed:** the suite went from 15 s to 44 s. The cause is real retry delays in the tool layer during mock runs. I've asked the ticket 07 agent to make those delays injectable and set them to zero in tests, without weakening any test.

Ticket 07 (single-agent baseline) is running. Once it merges, 09 (evaluation) and 11 (HTTP API) run in parallel, then 10 (LLM judge), then 13 (docs). Before 09 starts, decide whether inc07 and inc10 keep their current root-cause service names in the answer keys. Without an answer, 09 will score against them as they are.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T16:21:21.130Z

Ticket 11 is merged. `main` now has tickets 01–08, 11 and 12, with 287 tests passing and pyright clean.

**What 11 added:**
- **The API:** `uv run api` starts it on 127.0.0.1:8000, with Swagger at `/docs`.
  - `POST /investigations` starts a run, single- or multi-agent, and returns its id right away with a 202 status.
  - `GET /investigations/{id}` returns the run's status, its trace and, once finished, the report.
  - `GET /incidents` lists the 10 incidents.
- **Error handling:** an unknown incident or unknown investigation id returns 404, and a bad request body returns 422. A run that crashes ends as `failed` with an error message.
- **Storage:** every investigation is kept in memory and also saved as a JSON file. On restart, the API reloads the saved files and marks runs that were cut off as failed.
- **Inline incidents:** a report sent inline has no data behind it, so the tools find no evidence and the run ends INCONCLUSIVE.
- **Not covered:** there is no cancel endpoint. The trace only appears once a run finishes, not while it's running. The agent checked the server by hand but didn't run a real-LLM investigation through it.

I've told the ticket 09 agent (evaluation runner, still running) to pull in 11's changes before it commits. After 09 merges, I'll run ticket 10 (the LLM judge) and then ticket 13 (docs).

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T16:26:44.899Z

Ticket 09 is merged. `main` now has every ticket except 10 and 13. The suite passes (337 tests) and pyright is clean.

**What 09 added:**
- **Commands:**
  - `uv run eval --all` runs both systems on all 10 incidents.
  - `uv run eval --all --llm mock` runs offline.
- **Output:** results go to `eval/results/<run>/`, one file per incident and system plus `summary.md`. Each file is saved the moment its pair finishes, and a re-run skips pairs that already have a file.
- **Rate limits:** if your subscription hits a rate limit or usage cap, the runner stops cleanly, keeps the finished pairs, prints the exact resume command and exits with code 4.
- **Scoring:**
  - **Accuracy:** the top hypothesis must match both the service and the category. inc09 counts as correct only if the outcome is INCONCLUSIVE.
  - **Grounding:** this score also counts the evidence IDs the evidence rules removed. Otherwise every report would score 100%, because the rules strip bad IDs before the report is built.
- **Mock run:** all 20 pairs wrote, and a second run skipped all 20. The mock's accuracy figures mean nothing, because the mock doesn't actually reason.

**Known gaps:**
- Only the Claude CLI backend recognises rate limits; the API-key and local-model backends don't.
- An "overloaded" (HTTP 529) response stops the run instead of retrying.
- The rate-limit check matches the CLI's current error wording, which could change.
- inc07 and inc10 are scored strictly against `cart-svc` and `parcelhub-api`, with no partial credit for naming the calling service.

Ticket 10 (the LLM judge) is running now. Ticket 13 (README, architecture diagram, technical note, transcript) is last. It needs the live evaluation results, and running the live evaluation is your step.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T16:35:34.359Z

Ticket 10 is merged, so `main` now has every ticket except 13. All 357 tests pass and pyright is clean.

**What 10 added:**
- **The judge:** an Opus judge scores each report on evidence support, fact/assumption separation and recommended actions (1–5 each), and says whether the system fell for the trap. It never affects accuracy.
- **Commands:**
  - `uv run eval --all` now investigates, then judges, then writes the summary.
  - `--no-judge` skips the judge.
  - `--judge-only --run <name>` judges an existing run without investigating again.
- **Retries and failures:** bad judge output gets one retry. A second failure is recorded and the run continues. A recorded failure isn't retried on a re-run unless you clear it in the result file.
- **Cost:** the judge's tokens and calls go in a separate table in `summary.md`. They never count toward either system.
- **Saving and resume:** each judgement is saved into its pair's result file. A re-run skips pairs already judged, and the judge stops cleanly on a usage cap, like the runner.

Ticket 13 (README, architecture diagram, technical note, transcript notes) is running. It won't invent evaluation numbers. The README will show where the live results go, and that checkbox stays unticked until you run the live evaluation.

The live evaluation means 20 investigations plus 20 judge calls through your subscription. The one live multi-agent run so far (inc01) took about 77 s, so expect roughly 30 to 60 minutes. If you hit a usage cap, re-run the same command and it continues where it stopped. Do you want me to start it once ticket 13 merges, or would you rather run `uv run eval --all` yourself?

2. Key Technical Concepts:

   - **Multi-agent design:**
     - Blackboard = LangGraph state. It holds the incident report but never the Scenario, which lives only inside the per-run ToolSession.
     - Task DAG with parallel waves.
     - Agents: Orchestrator/planner (one Opus call), Observability, Dependency, Reasoning, Critic, Reporting.
   - **JSON action protocol:** `call_tool` or `finish`, validated by Pydantic.

   - **Tools:** five tools (logs, metrics, deploys + config changes, dependencies, past_incidents).
     - Stable evidence IDs of the form `<kind>:<incident>[:<series>]:NNNN`.
     - Deterministic failure injection (timeout, partial, error), plus 2 retries with backoff, then an EvidenceGap.
   - **Evidence rules** (deterministic):
     - A Fact citing unknown IDs is demoted to an Assumption.
     - A hypothesis with no supporting Fact is capped at 0.5.
     - Each affecting gap takes 0.1 off.
     - No hypothesis ≥ 0.6 gives INCONCLUSIVE.
     - Every change is logged as a RuleAdjustment.
   - **Limits** (`limits.py`, RunLimits/DEFAULT_LIMITS):
     - 6 tool calls per task, 30 per run.
     - 300k tokens (input + output, excluding cache reads).
     - 10 minutes.
     - 2 Critic loops.
     - `terminated_by` ∈ done | tool_budget | token_budget | timeout | critic_limit.
   - **Single-agent baseline:** same tools, limits, rules and schema. The 30-call budget is its binding limit.
   - **Evaluation:**
     - Deterministic accuracy (service + category; inc09 must be INCONCLUSIVE).
     - Grounding includes demoted and dropped IDs.
     - Atomic per-pair result files with resume.
     - LLMRateLimitError stops the run cleanly (exit 4) and prints the resume command.
   - **Opus judge:** support, separation and actions scored 1–5, plus fell_for_trap. One retry. Judge cost is kept separate.
   - **TDD:** red → green, vertical slices, tests at agreed seams.
   - **Agent workflow:** worktree-isolated agents (`isolation: "worktree"`), merged with `git merge --no-ff`.

     - `dataset.py`: `load_scenario`, `list_incident_ids`, `load_answer_key`, `UnknownIncidentError`, and the AnswerKey/Trap models. It is the only module besides `evaluation/` allowed to touch answer keys.

"""`anthropic_api` backend: the Messages API through the official SDK, paid by `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (Q15).

An optional switch away from the subscription. The published evaluation uses
`claude_cli`. Usage is mapped exactly as `claude_cli` maps it (D5).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import time
from typing import Any

import anthropic

from incident_investigator.llm.base import (
    CALL_TIMEOUT_S,
    IN_FLIGHT,
    LLMError,
    LLMResponse,
    LLMTimeoutError,
    LLMUnavailableError,
    Usage,
)

API_KEY_ENV = "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"
DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS = 4096


class AnthropicAPI:
    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        client: Any = None,
        timeout_s: int = CALL_TIMEOUT_S,
        max_tokens: int = DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS,
    ) -> None:
        """`client` is anything with `.messages.create(...)`; tests inject a fake."""
        self._client = client
        self.timeout_s = timeout_s
        self.max_tokens = max_tokens

    def preflight(self) -> None:
        if self._client is None and not os.environ.get(API_KEY_ENV):
            raise LLMUnavailableError(
                f"{API_KEY_ENV} is not set; the anthropic_api backend needs an API key. "
                "Export it, or use --llm claude_cli (subscription) or --llm mock."
            )

    def _sdk(self) -> Any:
        if self._client is None:
            self.preflight()
            # No SDK retries: a failed call is one failed attempt for the agent loop,
            # as with claude_cli.
            self._client = anthropic.Anthropic(
                api_key=os.environ[API_KEY_ENV], max_retries=0, timeout=self.timeout_s
            )
        return self._client

    def complete(self, *, system: str, prompt: str, model: str) -> LLMResponse:
        try:
            client = self._sdk()
        except LLMUnavailableError as exc:
            raise LLMError(str(exc)) from exc
        with IN_FLIGHT:
            started = time.monotonic()
            try:
                message = client.messages.create(
                    model=model,
                    max_tokens=self.max_tokens,
                    system=system,
                    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
                    timeout=self.timeout_s,
                )
            except anthropic.APITimeoutError as exc:
                raise LLMTimeoutError(f"anthropic_api call timed out after {self.timeout_s} s") from exc
            except anthropic.APIError as exc:
                raise LLMError(f"anthropic_api call failed: {exc}") from exc
            wall_s = time.monotonic() - started
        return _parse(message, wall_s)


def _count(usage: Any, name: str) -> int:
    return int(getattr(usage, name, 0) or 0)


def _parse(message: Any, wall_s: float) -> LLMResponse:
    text = "".join(block.text for block in message.content if getattr(block, "type", None) == "text")
    if not text:
        raise LLMError(f"anthropic_api reply has no text (stop_reason={getattr(message,

"""The anthropic_api backend, with a fake Anthropic client injected at the SDK boundary (Q15)."""

import threading
import time
from types import SimpleNamespace
from typing import Any

import anthropic
import httpx2
import pytest

from incident_investigator.llm.anthropic_api import AnthropicAPI
from incident_investigator.llm.base import LLMError, LLMTimeoutError, LLMUnavailableError

REQUEST = httpx2.Request("POST", "https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages")


def _message(text: str = '{"ok": true}', **usage: int | None) -> SimpleNamespace:
    counts: dict[str, int | None] = {"input_tokens": 5, "output_tokens": 9, "cache_creation_input_tokens": 700, "cache_read_input_tokens": 300}
    counts.update(usage)
    return SimpleNamespace(
        content=[SimpleNamespace(type="text", text=text)],
        usage=SimpleNamespace(**counts),
        stop_reason="end_turn",
    )


class FakeMessages:
    def __init__(self, reply: Any = None, *, delay_s: float = 0.0) -> None:
        self.reply = reply if reply is not None else _message()
        self.delay_s = delay_s
        self.calls: list[dict[str, Any]] = []

    def create(self, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        self.calls.append(kwargs)
        if self.delay_s:
            time.sleep(self.delay_s)
        if isinstance(self.reply, Exception):
            raise self.reply
        return self.reply


def _api(reply: Any = None, **kwargs: Any) -> tuple[AnthropicAPI, FakeMessages]:
    messages = FakeMessages(reply, **kwargs)
    return AnthropicAPI(client=SimpleNamespace(messages=messages)), messages


def _complete(api: AnthropicAPI):
    return api.complete(system="sys", prompt="the prompt", model="claude-haiku-5-5")


def test_a_valid_reply_becomes_an_llm_response_with_usage_mapped_like_claude_cli():
    api, _ = _api()

    response = _complete(api)

    assert response.text == '{"ok": true}'
    # tokens_in = input + cache writes; cache reads are reported apart (D5).
    assert (response.usage.tokens_in, response.usage.tokens_out, response.usage.cache_tokens) == (705, 9, 300)
    assert response.latency_s >= 0


def test_missing_cache_counts_are_read_as_zero():
    api, _ = _api(_message(cache_creation_input_tokens=None, cache_read_input_tokens=None))

    usage = _complete(api).usage

    assert (usage.tokens_in, usage.cache_tokens) == (5, 0)


def test_the_request_passes_the_system_prompt_as_system_and_the_prompt_as_one_user_turn():
    api, messages = _api()

    _complete(api)

    call = messages.calls[0]
    assert call["model"] == "claude-haiku-5-5"
    assert call["system"] == "sys"
    assert call["messages"] == [{"role": "user", "content": "the prompt"}]
    assert call["max_tokens"] > 0
    assert call["timeout"] == 120


def test_text_blocks_are_joined_and_other_blocks_ignored():
    reply = _message()
    reply.content = [
        SimpleNamespace(type="thinking", thinking="hmm"),
        SimpleNamespace(type="text", text='{"a":'),
        SimpleNamespace(type="text", text=" 1}"),
    ]

"""Per-role backend routing from the models config (ticket 12, Q20)."""

from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from incident_investigator.llm.anthropic_api import AnthropicAPI
from incident_investigator.llm.base import LLMError, LLMResponse, LLMUnavailableError, Usage
from incident_investigator.llm.claude_cli import ClaudeCLI
from incident_investigator.llm.mock import MockLLM
from incident_investigator.llm.openai_compat import OpenAICompat
from incident_investigator.llm.router import RoleRouter, make_client
from incident_investigator.models_config import ModelsConfig, ModelsConfigError, load_models

ROLES = ["planner", "reasoning", "critic", "baseline", "judge", "observability", "dependency", "reporting"]


class Fake:
    def __init__(self, name: str, *, unavailable: bool = False) -> None:
        self.name = name
        self.unavailable = unavailable
        self.preflights = 0
        self.calls: list[str] = []

    def preflight(self) -> None:
        self.preflights += 1
        if self.unavailable:
            raise LLMUnavailableError(f"{self.name} is down")

    def complete(self, *, system: str, prompt: str, model: str) -> LLMResponse:
        self.calls.append(model)
        return LLMResponse(text=self.name, usage=Usage(tokens_in=1))


def _config(tmp_path: Path, **overrides: str) -> ModelsConfig:
    lines = [f"{role} = {overrides.get(role, '{ backend = \"claude_cli\", model = \"opus\" }')}" for role in ROLES]
    path = tmp_path / "models.toml"
    path.write_text("[roles]\n" + "\n".join(lines) + "\n")
    return load_models(path)


def _mixed(tmp_path: Path) -> ModelsConfig:
    return _config(
        tmp_path,
        observability='{ backend = "openai_compat", model = "qwen" }',
        judge='{ backend = "anthropic_api", model = "api-opus" }',
    )


def _fakes() -> dict[str, Fake]:
    return {name: Fake(name) for name in ("claude_cli", "anthropic_api", "openai_compat", "mock")}


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("backend", "cls"),
    [("claude_cli", ClaudeCLI), ("anthropic_api", AnthropicAPI), ("openai_compat", OpenAICompat), ("mock", MockLLM)],
)
def test_the_factory_builds_each_known_backend(backend: str, cls: type):
        '{"action": "finish", "facts": [{"statement": "no ids", "evidence_ids": []}]}',
        '{"action": "dance"}',
        '{"action": "call_tool", "tool": "logs", "args": {}, "extra": 1}',
    ],
)
def test_invalid_actions_raise_action_parse_error(text: str):
    with pytest.raises(ActionParseError):
        parse_action(text)


def _cli_reply(result: str) -> str:
    return json.dumps(
        {
            "type": "result",
            "is_error": False,
            "duration_ms": 500,
            "result": result,
            "usage": {"input_tokens": 10, "output_tokens": 5, "cache_read_input_tokens": 100},
        }
    )


class ScriptedCLI:
    """subprocess.run stand-in that replays model replies in order."""

You need:

- macOS or Linux.
- [uv](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/). uv installs Python 3.12 for you if it is missing (`requires-python = ">=3.12,<3.13"`).
- For live runs: the Claude Code CLI (`claude`), logged in with a Claude subscription. This is the default LLM backend. It was tested with Claude Code 2.1.322.

Steps:

1. Install the dependencies (the versions are locked in `uv.lock`):

   ```sh
   uv sync
   ```

2. Log in to the Claude CLI once, if you have not already. Run `claude`, then use `/login` and choose your subscription. Check it with:

   ```sh
   claude --version
   ```

3. Check the install without using any LLM. The `mock` backend is an offline stand-in:

   ```sh
   uv run investigate inc01 --llm mock
   ```

   It prints `ROOT_CAUSE: report written to runs/inc01__multi.json`.

You do not need an API key. Two other backends are optional:

- **Anthropic API:** set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and use `--llm anthropic_api`.
- **A local model** (Ollama, LM Studio, vLLM) through an OpenAI-compatible endpoint: use `--llm openai_compat`, or set `backend = "openai_compat"` for single roles in `src/incident_investigator/models.toml`. The base URL comes from `$OPENAI_COMPAT_BASE_URL`, then `[backends.openai_compat] base_url` in `models.toml`, then `http://localhost:11434/v1`. `$OPENAI_COMPAT_API_KEY` is sent as a bearer token when it is set.

### Models

`src/incident_investigator/models.toml` sets the backend and model for each role. The defaults (DECISIONS.md Q8):

| Role | Model |
|---|---|
| planner (Orchestrator), reasoning, critic, baseline, judge | `claude-opus-5-5` |
| observability, dependency, reporting | `claude-haiku-5-5` |

All roles use the `claude_cli` backend by default. `--llm <backend>` on the CLI or the evaluation overrides the backend for every role.

### How the Claude CLI is called

Each LLM call runs `claude -p --output-format json` with a replacement system prompt, no built-in tools, no user or project settings, no hooks, no MCP servers and no saved session. The working directory is outside the project. See `docs/adr/0001-claude-cli-isolation-flags.md` for the flags and the evidence that they work with subscription login. At most 3 LLM calls run at the same time, and each call has a 120 s timeout.

### Key dependency versions (from `uv.lock`)

| Package | Version |
|---|---|
| Python | 3.12 (tested with 3.12.16) |
| langgraph | 1.2.20 |
| pydantic | 2.14.3 |
| fastapi | 0.162.5 |
| uvicorn | 0.54.0 |
| anthropic | 2.8.0 |
| httpx | 0.28.1 |
| pytest (dev) | 9.2.0 |
| pyright (dev) | 1.1.414 |

- `incident_id`: `inc01` to `inc10` (the files in `data/incidents/`).
- `--mode multi` (default): the multi-agent graph. `--mode single`: the single-agent baseline.
- `--llm`: the backend for every role. Without it, each role uses its backend from `models.toml` (`claude_cli`

<!-- DRAFT by the coding assistant: the project owner edits and signs off this note before submission. -->

# Technical note

## Key decisions

- **LangGraph state as a blackboard.** Six agents never message each other. They write typed Pydantic entries (findings, hypotheses, gaps, failures) to shared state, and reducers merge parallel writes.
- **Hybrid Orchestrator.** One Opus call plans a task DAG; plain Python validates it and runs it in parallel waves. Scheduling, retries and limits stay deterministic.
- **Claude through the subscription CLI.** With no API key, every call runs `claude -p` with flags that strip tools, settings, hooks and MCP servers (ADR 0001). The CLI has no native tool calling, so agents reply with Pydantic-validated JSON actions.
- **Code, not the LLM, owns confidence.** Facts must cite evidence IDs a tool actually returned. Unsupported hypotheses are capped at 0.5, each evidence gap subtracts 0.1, and below 0.6 the outcome is `INCONCLUSIVE`.
- **Reproducible failures.** Scenarios inject timeouts, partial results and errors on the Nth call to a tool. A turnstile fixes the tool-call order across parallel agents, so the same call fails on every run.
- **A fair baseline.** One Opus agent with the same tools, failure schedule, limits, evidence rules and report schema.
- **Deterministic accuracy.** Accuracy and grounding come from answer keys. An Opus judge scores reasoning quality but never decides accuracy; its cost is reported separately.

## Trade-offs

- The multi-agent system makes more LLM calls than the baseline, so it pays the per-call overhead more often.
- Without native tool calling, the baseline re-sends its transcript each step, so its tokens grow quickly with tool calls.
- Hand-written incidents give reliable answer keys, but only 10, written alongside the system they test.

## Limitations

- **CLI overhead and token accounting.** Each `claude -p` call has about 1 s of start-up and fixed overhead the flags cannot remove; the multi-agent system pays it on every call. Cache writes count as input tokens; cache reads are reported separately and excluded from the budget.
- **One run per incident,** without significance tests.
- **The judge is a Claude model,** like the systems it grades, and may favour their style. It also sees the answer key.
- The live evaluation was still pending when this was drafted.
- Only the CLI backend detects rate limits.
- Evidence gaps never close, even when a retry recovers the data.
- inc07 and inc10 have contestable answer keys, with no credit for a neighbouring service.
- The API cannot cancel a run or stream its trace.

## Possible improvements

- Repeat each incident and report variance.
- Use native tool calling and prompt caching through the API to cut per-call overhead.
- Close gaps when evidence is recovered; give partial credit for adjacent services.
- Add a judge from another model family, calibrated against human ratings.
- Add incidents written by someone other than the build
… [truncated 4786 chars]
```

# 12: Optional LLM backends (API key and local model)

**What to build:** The system can run without the subscription. Two more `LLMClient` backends are added: `anthropic_api` (reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) and `openai_compat` (Ollama, LM Studio or vLLM through an OpenAI-compatible endpoint). Each is selectable per agent in the models config. The published evaluation still uses Claude through the CLI. This is the first ticket to cut if time runs short.

**Blocked by:** 02 (Claude CLI backend)

**Status:** done

Context: PRD capability map, non-goals. `DECISIONS.md` Q15, Q20.

- [x] Both backends implement the same interface and return the same JSON action and usage fields as `claude_cli`
- [x] Choosing a backend per agent in the models config works. An unknown backend name fails at startup with a clear message
- [x] A missing API key or an unreachable local endpoint fails fast with a clear message
- [x] Both are covered by mocked tests. A live local-model run is optional and documented if done

## Comments

**2026-11-12, implementation:**
- **Backends:** `llm/anthropic_api.py` (`AnthropicAPI`, official `anthropic` SDK 2.8.0) and `llm/openai_compat.py` (`OpenAICompat`, plain `httpx` 0.28.1, `POST {base}/chat/completions`, no openai SDK). Both implement `preflight()` and `complete(*, system, prompt, model) -> LLMResponse`. The model's reply text goes back unchanged, so the JSON action protocol is the same as for `claude_cli`. Errors: a timeout raises `LLMTimeoutError`, an API, HTTP or connection error or a malformed reply raises `LLMError`.
- **Usage mapping (D5):** `anthropic_api` maps usage exactly like `claude_cli`: `tokens_in` = `input_tokens` + `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_tokens` = `cache_read_input_tokens`. `openai_compat` maps `tokens_out` = `completion_tokens`. When the server reports `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` (Ollama and vLLM do), those tokens go to `cache_tokens` and `tokens_in` = `prompt_tokens` − cached. So cache reads stay out of the budget for every backend.
- **Limits:** the 120 s timeout and the semaphore of 3 moved from `claude_cli.py` to `llm/base.py` (`CALL_TIMEOUT_S`, `MAX_IN_FLIGHT`, `IN_FLIGHT`). `claude_cli` re-exports the names. The cap of 3 now covers all backends together, process-wide (Q20). `anthropic_api` sets the SDK's `max_retries=0`, so the agent loop owns retries, as with `claude_cli`.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T19:08:31.072Z

The subscription backend is gone. Claude is now called only through `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and that's the default for every agent. Committed as `a46c1c4`. 333 tests pass and pyright is clean; the count dropped from 359 because the deleted backend's tests went with it.

**One thing went wrong:** while I was rewriting the tests, a test ran against the old default before I had changed the code. Your CLI was logged in, so it may have sent a few real `claude -p` calls on your subscription before I stopped it. No `claude -p` processes are left running. To stop anything like this happening with your API key, every test now runs with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` unset. Tests that need a key use a fake one and a fake API client, so the suite can never make a billed call.

**What changed:**
- **Removed:** the CLI backend and its tests, and the `--llm claude_cli` option. A config that still names that backend now fails at startup with a clear error.
- **Default backend:** the API backend for every agent, in `models.toml`.
- **Missing key:** a live run, the HTTP API or the evaluation fails straight away with exit code 3 and writes nothing.
- **Rate limits:** the API backend now spots them. A `429` stops the evaluation cleanly and it can pick up where it left off. A `529` overload is now retried instead of stopping the run. Both were open issues before.
- **Docs:** the README, technical note and architecture diagram now describe the API key setup instead of the subscription, and the CLI-overhead caveats are gone. I added a decision record for the switch (ADR 0002, which replaces ADR 0001), a new entry in `DECISIONS.md` and a ticket 14 for this change.

**Still to know:**
- The API backend has only been tested with a fake client and has never made a real call. The first real check is `export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...` then `uv run investigate inc01`.
- The live evaluation will now be billed to your key. Each investigation is capped at 300k tokens.
- I left the PRD and the old tickets 02 and 12 as they were; they still describe the subscription route.
- The session transcripts are still uncommitted for your privacy review.

# Multi-Agent Production Incident Investigator

Sources: `orange_invest_assignment.md` (the brief) and `DECISIONS.md` (Q1–Q24, the decision record).
This PRD adds the interview of 2026-11-12 (L24–L26) and recommendations still awaiting sign-off (D#).

## TL;DR

A Python CLI and API that investigates simulated production incidents with six cooperating agents. The agents share a typed blackboard and a task DAG, and a Critic can send the investigation back for more evidence. The system returns ranked, evidence-cited hypotheses, or an honest `INCONCLUSIVE`.

The thesis is to reuse LangGraph for the control flow and the Claude Code CLI for the LLM, and to build only the investigation logic, the simulated data, and a fair evaluation against a single-agent baseline.

The audience is the reviewer of the AI Engineer assignment. The budget is one overnight build (Q3).

## Locked assumptions

- **Language/runtime:** Python 3.12, managed with `uv` (Q1).
- **Framework:** LangGraph (Q14). FastAPI for the API (Q19). Pydantic v2 for all schemas. *Exact package versions are pinned in `uv.lock` at scaffold time (D9).*
- **LLM access:** Claude through the user's subscription via `claude -p --output-format json` (Q15). There is no API key today (Q13).
- **Models:** Opus 5.5 for plan creation, Reasoning, Critic, the baseline and the judge. Haiku 5.5 for Observability, Dependency and Reporting (Q8).
- **Deployment:** local only, on the developer's macOS machine. No hosting, no database, no remote git (Q19, Q22).
|---|---|---|
| Graph runtime, typed state, parallel fan-out (`Send`), conditional edges, `recursion_limit` | `[core]` | LangGraph as-is |
| LLM access via subscription | `[core]` | `claude` CLI 2.1.322 (installed, verified 2026-11-12) |
| API server + Swagger at `/docs` | `[core]` | FastAPI as-is |
| Schema validation | `[core]` | Pydantic v2 |
| `LLMClient` interface + 3 backends (`claude_cli` default, `anthropic_api`, `openai_compat`) | `[build]` | New. Per-agent choice in `models.yaml` (Q15, Q20). At most 3 concurrent LLM calls. |
| `claude_cli` overhead stripping | `[build]` | New. Replacement system prompt, `--tools ""`, no user/project settings, hooks or MCP servers (L24, R2) |
| Synthetic incident dataset (10) + answer keys | `[build]` | New. JSON scenarios with 2–3 traps each (Q18). Keys in a separate folder that agents never read. |
| 3 | `LLMClient` call | `pytest tests/test_llm.py` (mocked subprocess) passes. Valid JSON is parsed into an action. **Invalid JSON twice** produces an `AgentFailure`, and the run continues. The CLI command line contains `--tools ""`, a replacement system prompt and the settings/MCP isolation flags (R2). Token fields are read from the CLI's JSON `usage`. |
- **Per investigation, for both systems (L26):** a 300,000-token budget (input plus output, excluding cache reads), a 10-minute wall clock, and 30 tool calls.
- **Per LLM call:** a 120 s timeout. A timeout counts as one failed attempt toward the agent-failure limit of 2

# Triage Labels

The skills speak in terms of five canonical triage roles. This file maps those roles to the actual label strings used in this repo's issue tracker.

| Label in mattpocock/skills | Label in our tracker | Meaning                                  |
| -------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| `needs-triage`             | `needs-triage`       | Maintainer needs to evaluate this issue  |
| `needs-info`               | `needs-info`         | Waiting on reporter for more information |
| `ready-for-agent`          | `ready-for-agent`    | Fully specified, ready for an AFK agent  |
| `ready-for-human`          | `ready-for-human`    | Requires human implementation            |
| `wontfix`                  | `wontfix`            | Will not be actioned                     |

When a skill mentions a role (e.g. "apply the AFK-ready triage label"), use the corresponding label string from this table.

Edit the "Label in our tracker" column to match whatever vocabulary you actually use.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T19:19:21.456Z

I checked this run and the three API runs before it.

**Verdict:** the agents found the right root cause, but the report says INCONCLUSIVE. A known open issue knocked the confidence just below the threshold. The evaluation would score this run as wrong.

**What went right:**
- **Right answer ranked first:** h1 is the checkout-svc v2.3.1 "Discount engine refactor" deploy causing a NullPointerException in `applyDiscount`. That matches the answer key's service and category.
- **All 4 key evidence IDs cited:** `deploy:inc01:0001`, `log:inc01:0003`, `log:inc01:0004` and `metric:inc01:err:0002`. No evidence ID it cited is missing from the incident data.
- **Both traps avoided:** the CPU noisy-neighbour theory came last at 0.0, and the agents argued against it with the NullPointerException evidence. The payments-svc TLS red herring never came up as a hypothesis.
- **The machinery worked as designed:**
  - All 4 planned tasks ran in parallel; the trace shows them starting within the same 5 ms.
  - The Critic ran its 2 allowed loops with 5 follow-up tasks, then was satisfied, so it ended with `terminated_by: done`.
  - No agent failures.
  - The recommended actions are sensible: roll back v2.3.1, then re-pull the missing data.
- **Cost:** 27 tool calls, 45 LLM calls, 132k input and 42k output tokens, about 4 minutes. That fits inside the 30-call, 300k-token and 10-minute limits. Cache tokens were 0, so prompt caching isn't in use.

**Why it came out INCONCLUSIVE:**
1. In task t3, a metrics query about pricing-db timed out three times. That timeout is a planned failure built into this incident's data. It opened an evidence gap.
2. In the Critic's first loop, task c1.2 re-ran those pricing-db metrics. The tool answered `ok` with no rows, and that agent noted the timeout didn't happen again.
3. Gaps never close, so the old timeout still counted. It also took 0.1 off every hypothesis, including h1, which is about checkout-svc and not pricing-db. h1 went from 0.62 to 0.52, under the 0.6 threshold.
4. The Critic's final review even says "0.52 likely understates it".

So the open issue "evidence gaps never close" isn't a minor caveat. It flipped a correct run to the wrong outcome, and it will hurt the live evaluation the same way.

**The three earlier runs** (`de18e161`, `934c1eba`, `73e4a05b`) all failed on authentication:
- The first two went through the old subscription backend and got "Invalid API key". Your subscription problem was most likely an invalid `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the environment; Claude Code uses that key instead of the logged-in subscription when the variable is set.
- The third used the API and got `401 Invalid bearer token`. 36b674 then succeeded, so a valid key was in place by then.

That exposes a second bug: a wrong key doesn't stop the run. Each run made 8 failed calls and then saved a "done" report saying INCONCLUSIVE, which looks like a real result. In the evaluation, a wrong key would fill all 20 result files with fake INCONCLUSIVE results.

**Fixes I'd make, both test-first:**
1. **Close a gap when the same tool later succeeds** for the same service. With that rule this run would have kept 0.62 and been scored correct.
2. **Treat a 401 or 403 from the API as fatal,** like a rate limit. The run stops with a clear error instead of saving a report, and the evaluation exits so it can be resumed after the key is fixed.

Should I go ahead with both?

"""The deterministic evidence rules between the investigation and the report (ticket 05,
PRD step 7, Q16, Q21). Code, never the LLM, decides what counts as a Fact and how far
each Hypothesis's confidence may go.

`apply_evidence_rules(board)` reads only the blackboard (never the Scenario), so the
single-agent baseline (ticket 07) calls it on its own blackboard. It is pure: it reads
the agents' raw entries (`proposed_confidence`, the cited IDs) and never its own output,
so running it twice gives the same result.

Definitions:
- An evidence ID **exists** when a tool returned that row during this run, i.e. it is
  in `board.evidence`. An ID that is in the dataset but was never returned does not count.
- A Fact that cites any ID that does not exist is not kept as a Fact. It becomes an
  Assumption whose basis names the invalid and valid IDs. Nothing is dropped silently.
- Hypothesis supporting/contradicting IDs that do not exist are dropped.
- A Hypothesis has a **supporting Fact** when at least one kept Fact (after the check
  above) cites at least one of its (cleaned) `supporting_ids`. Without one, its
  confidence is capped at 0.5 (`UNSUPPORTED_CAP`).
- Every EvidenceGap on the blackboard is **open**: nothing closes a gap yet (a later
  successful query does not prove the missing rows are back).
- A gap **affects** a Hypothesis when the gap names it in `affects_hypotheses`. A gap
  that names none affects every Hypothesis whose category its tool could confirm or
  refute (`TOOL_COVERS`): logs and metrics cover every cause; deploys (deployments
  and config changes) covers change-driven causes; dependencies covers causes outside
  the service; past_incidents covers none, because history is not evidence about this
  incident. The report's gaps carry the resolved `affects_hypotheses`.
- Each affecting open gap lowers the confidence by 0.1 (`GAP_PENALTY`), after the cap,
  with a floor of 0. Confidences are rounded to 2 decimals.
- `final_confidence` = `proposed_confidence`, then the cap, then the gap penalties.
- The outcome is ROOT_CAUSE when some hypothesis's final confidence is at least 0.6
  (`CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD`), else INCONCLUSIVE. Either way the report lists the ranked
  hypotheses and the gaps.
- A recommended action whose `hypothesis_id` is not a reported hypothesis is dropped.
- Every such change is a `RuleAdjustment` in `EvidenceReview.adjustments` and in the
  report's `rule_adjustments`.

Both modes finish the same way: fill a Blackboard, call `apply_evidence_rules(board)`
to get the ranked hypotheses (for the Reporting LLM), then
`build_report(board, review=..., actions=..., metrics=..., terminated_by=...)`.
"""

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict

from incident_investigator.schemas import (

    hypotheses: list[Hypot

"""Tool registry and dispatcher, with deterministic failure injection (Q5, Q16).

Agents call tools by name with a raw args dict. `ToolSession.call` validates the
args into the tool's typed input, runs the tool and applies the scenario's
`failure_schedule`. It never raises: bad input comes back as `status=error`.

Create one `ToolSession` per investigation run. It owns the per-tool call
counters, so the Nth call to a tool fails the same way on every run.

Failure semantics:
- The schedule counts accepted calls per tool, starting at 1. A call rejected as
  invalid input never reached the "backend", so it does not count.
- `timeout`: the scheduled call keeps timing out, so it is retried `retries` times
  with exponential backoff (backoff_s, 2*backoff_s, ...), then returns
  `status=timeout`, `attempts=retries+1` and an EvidenceGap.
- `partial`: the first floor(n * partial_fraction) rows (default fraction 0.5),
  `status=partial`, `omitted` set, and an EvidenceGap.
- `error`: `status=error` and an EvidenceGap. Not retried.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
import threading
import time
from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, cast, get_args

from pydantic import BaseModel, ValidationError

    FailureSpec,

    ToolName,

)

from incident_investigator.tools.logs import LogsQuery, query_logs
from incident_investigator.tools.metrics import MetricsQuery, query_metrics
from incident_investigator.tools.past_incidents import PastIncidentsQuery, query_past_incidents

DEFAULT_PARTIAL_FRACTION = 0.5
DEFAULT_BACKOFF_S = 0.5
"""Base backoff before the first retry of a timed-out call; it doubles per retry."""

class ToolSpec:

def build_report(
    board: Blackboard,
    *,
    actions: list[RecommendedAction],
    metrics: ReportMetrics,
    terminated_by: TerminatedBy,
    review: EvidenceReview | None = None,
) -> InvestigationReport:
    """The shared report assembly for both investigation modes.

    Applies the evidence rules (or uses `review`, the result of `apply_evidence_rules`
    on this same board, to avoid computing it twice), links every action to a reported
    hypothesis, and sets the outcome: ROOT_CAUSE when some hypothesis's final
    confidence reaches `CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD`, else INCONCLUSIVE."""
    review = review or apply_evidence_rules(board)
    kept, dropped = link_actions(actions, review.hypotheses)
    top = max((h.final_confidence or 0.0 for h in review.hypotheses), default=0.0)
    return InvestigationReport(
        incident_id=board.incident_id,
        outcome="ROOT_CAUSE" if top >= CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD else "INCONCLUSIVE",
        ranked_hypotheses=review.hypotheses,
        facts=review.facts,
        assumptions=review.assumptions,
        recommended_actions=kept,
        gaps=review.gaps,
        metrics=metrics,
        terminated_by=terminated_by,
        rule_adjustments=review.adjustments + dropped,
    )

# --- A rate limit or usage cap is not an ordinary failed attempt (ticket 09, L25) ----


def _rate_limited(_: str) -> Any:
    raise LLMRateLimitError("You've hit your limit")


@pytest.mark.parametrize("role", ["planner", "observability", "dependency", "reasoning", "critic", "reporting"])
def test_a_rate_limit_in_any_multi_agent_role_propagates_out_of_investigate(role: str):
    with pytest.raises(LLMRateLimitError):
        investigate(INCIDENT, RoleLLM(delay_s=0.0, **{role: _rate_limited}), tool_backoff_s=0)


def test_a_rate_limit_in_the_baseline_propagates_out_of_investigate():
    with pytest.raises(LLMRateLimitError):
        investigate(INCIDENT, RoleLLM(delay_s=0.0, baseline=_rate_limited), mode="single", tool_backoff_s=0)


def test_an_ordinary_llm_error_is_still_a_failed_attempt_not_a_crash():

        raise LLMError("bad gateway")

    run = investigate(INCIDENT, RoleLLM(delay_s=0.0, observability=broken), tool_backoff_s=0)

    assert any(f.task_id == "t1" for f in run.report.failures)

def test_a_pair_cut_off_midway_leaves_no_file_and_keeps_the_completed_ones(tmp_path: Path):
    killed = FailingOnRole("baseline", KeyboardInterrupt(), nth=3)

    with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt):
        run_eval(killed, tmp_path, incident_ids=IDS[:2], llm_label="mock")

    assert result_files(tmp_path) == [f"{IDS[0]}__multi.json"]
    assert not list(tmp_path.glob("*.tmp"))


def test_an_investigation_that_raises_leaves_no_file_for_that_pair(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch):
    real = runner.investigate

    def crash_on_second_single(incident_id: str, llm: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        if incident_id == IDS[1] and kwargs.get("mode") == "single":
            raise RuntimeError("crashed midway")
        return real(incident_id, llm, **kwargs)

    monkeypatch.setattr(runner, "investigate", crash_on_second_single)

    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="crashed midway"):
        run_eval(MockLLM(delay_s=0.0), tmp_path, incident_ids=IDS[:3], llm_label="mock")

    assert result_files(tmp_path) == [f"{IDS[0]}__multi.json", f"{IDS[0]}__single.json", f"{IDS[1]}__multi.json"]


def test_a_rate_limit_stops_cleanly_and_keeps_the_completed_pairs(tmp_path: Path):
    capped = FailingOnRole("baseline", LLMRateLimitError("You've hit your limit"))

    assert run.rate_limited is not None and "hit your limit" in run.rate_limited
    assert [p.name for p in run.written] == [f"{IDS[0]}__multi.json"]
    assert result_files(tmp_path) == [f"{IDS[0]}__multi.json"]
    assert not list(tmp_path.glob("*.tmp"))
    assert "1 of 4" in (tmp_path / "summary.md").read_text()

    out = capsys.readouterr()

    resume = f"uv run eval --incidents {IDS[0]},{IDS[1]} --llm mock --results-dir {tmp_path} --run r2"
    assert resume in out.out + out.err
    assert result_files(tmp_path / "r2") == [f"{IDS[0]}__multi.json"]

baseline.py:3:It shares everything with the multi-agent graph except the architecture:
api.py:115:            except ValidationError:
api.py:180:        except Exception as exc:  # any crash must end as `failed`, never a hang
and an ordinary `LLMError` (timeout, crash) are both failed attempts. After the last
one the pair is recorded as a judge failure (`status = "failed"`) and the run goes on.
`LLMFatalError` (a rate limit or a rejected key) is not a failed attempt: it propagates so the runner can stop
cleanly and print the resume command.

**Cost.** The judge's LLM calls, tokens and latency are kept in `JudgeScores`, never in
the report's metrics, so they are not charged to either system (PRD D6).
15:On a rate limit or usage cap (`LLMRateLimitError`) the runner stops, keeps the

    written: list[Path] = field(default_factory=list[Path])
    skipped: list[Path] = field(default_factory=list[Path])
    judged: list[Path] = field(default_factory=list[Path])
    """Result files the judge wrote a verdict or a judge failure into during this call."""
    judge_failed: list[Path] = field(default_factory=list[Path])
    rate_limited: str | None = None
    """The rate-limit / usage-cap message that stopped the run, if one did."""
    summary: Path | None = None


def result_path(run_dir: Path, incident_id: str, system: str) -> Path:

def write_atomically(path: Path, text: str) -> None:

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T19:30:19.624Z

Both fixes are in and committed as `a2034a6`. All 352 tests pass and pyright is clean.

**1. A recovered evidence gap now closes.** When a tool query fails, the run records an evidence gap. If a later query to the same tool for the same service succeeds, that gap now closes. A closed gap no longer lowers any confidence, is left out of the report's gaps, and is logged in the report's rule adjustments as `gap_closed`. A partial result doesn't close a gap. Agents now see only the gaps that are still open. This works the same in multi-agent mode and the single-agent baseline.

In your run, the Critic's re-run of the pricing-db metrics should now close the t3 timeout. That would keep h1 at 0.62, a ROOT_CAUSE on the right answer. I couldn't confirm this, because the blackboard doesn't store tool arguments and I can't see which service the re-run queried. The Critic's goal says it re-ran "the timed-out pricing-db metrics", so it most likely matches. A fresh `uv run investigate inc01` is the real check.

One trade-off: a gap closes on any successful query for that service, even over a different time window or metric. A stricter rule matching the exact query would not have closed the gap in your run, because the re-run asked for narrower metrics. I listed this in the README's unfinished features.

**2. A rejected API key now stops the run.** A `401` or `403` stops the run at the first call, the same way a rate limit already does. That stops a bad key from producing a fake INCONCLUSIVE report:
- **CLI:** exits 3 with an error naming `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and writes no report.
- **HTTP API:** the run ends as `failed` with that error, not `done`.
- **Evaluation:** it stops and saves nothing for the rejected incident. It prints the resume command and exits 3. A bad key can no longer fill the results with fake INCONCLUSIVE reports.

I updated the README and technical note, and recorded both fixes in a new ticket 15. The session transcripts are still uncommitted, waiting for your privacy review.
