### Assistant · 2026-11-12T19:40:51.868Z

This run is incident **inc04**, not inc01. It ranked the correct answer first (image-resizer, `capacity`), but the report says INCONCLUSIVE, so the evaluation would score it wrong. This time the evidence rules didn't cause it, and the gap fix worked as intended. The run lost most of its tool budget to two failed tasks, so it never gathered the evidence that proves the cause.

**What went right**
- **Correct top hypothesis:** h1 is image-resizer `capacity`: heap at the 4 GiB ceiling, long garbage-collection pauses and 3 out-of-memory kills.
- **Gap fix confirmed on a live run:** inc04's data makes the first two log queries time out (`gap:1` and `gap:2`). Later log queries for image-resizer succeeded, and both gaps were closed and logged as `gap_closed`. No confidence was taken off.
- **Every evidence ID it cited exists in the data.**
- **Traps:** the thumbnail-cache deploy trap came second (h4 at 0.25). The top hypothesis doesn't cite any trap evidence. The oversized-upload trap never became a hypothesis.

**Why it ended INCONCLUSIVE**
No rule lowered anything: h1's confidence was 0.45 when the reasoning agent proposed it and still 0.45 at the end. The agent itself wasn't confident, because the evidence was thin.
- **It never found the decisive evidence.** The answer key's main clue is `metric:inc04:mem:0001`: memory had been climbing for five days, while request rate stayed flat. The agents only queried metrics inside the incident window, so they never saw that row. They also never retrieved `log:inc04:0004`. They did retrieve `log:inc04:0005`, but no hypothesis cited it.
- **The Critic asked for exactly that check too late.** In its second review it asked whether memory was already climbing before the deploy (task c2.1), and it said "h1 describes a mechanism, not a root cause". By then the run had used all 30 tool calls, so every task in that second round of Critic follow-ups failed with 0 tool calls. The run ended with `terminated_by: tool_budget`, and the final reasoning pass (c2.r) never ran.

**Where the budget went:** 12 of the 30 tool calls produced nothing.
- **t2 (deploys and config changes):** it made 6 tool calls, reached the per-task limit and kept trying to call tools. It failed, and everything it found was thrown away.
- **c1.1 (does each 502 burst line up with a kill):** it made 6 tool calls, then sent a final answer in which three facts cited no evidence IDs. The whole answer was rejected twice, so all 6 calls were lost.
- **c1.2 (blob-store theory):** it spent 6 calls on blob-store queries that all came back empty.

**Fixes I'd make, all test-first.** These are design issues, not crashes:
1. **When a task reaches its tool limit, give it one final answer-only turn** instead of failing. t2's 6 calls would then still produce findings.
2. **Don't reject a whole answer because some facts cite no evidence.** Keep the valid facts and move the uncited ones to assumptions, as the evidence rules already do for facts that cite unknown IDs. c1.1's 6 calls would then count.
3. **Tell the Critic how many tool calls are left,** and don't start follow-up tasks when the budget is already gone. Right now the Critic doesn't see the remaining budget. The single-agent baseline's prompt already includes its budget.

Fixes 1 and 2 alone would have recovered 12 calls, likely enough for the Critic's second round, including the check for the five-day memory climb. Fix 3 changes agent behaviour, so its effect only shows in live runs. Should I go ahead with all three?

"""The agent loop: ask the model for an action, run tools in Python, repeat until finish.

The CLI backend has no native tool calling (Q15), so each step re-sends the whole
transcript as one prompt. Limits (from `limits.RunLimits`): 6 tool calls per task,
2 failed attempts (invalid reply, LLM error or timeout, tool call over the limit)
-> AgentFailure. A run limit (`RunStopped` from the LLM or a tool) ends the loop
cleanly: the run keeps the agent's evidence and usage, with no finish and no failure.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any

)

MAX_FAILED_ATTEMPTS = DEFAULT_LIMITS.max_failed_attempts

Tool = Callable[[dict[str, Any]], ToolResult]


@dataclass
class AgentRun:
    finish: FinishAction | None = None
    failure: AgentFailure | None = None
    evidence: list[Evidence] = field(default_factory=list[Evidence])
    tool_calls: int = 0
    llm_calls: int = 0
    usage: Usage = field(default_factory=Usage)
    llm_latency_s: float = 0.0
    stopped: bool = False
    """A run limit stopped this task before it finished."""


def run_agent(
    *,
    llm: LLMClient,

    `check_finish` may reject a finish action with a reason (for example, too few

    the model. `max_tool_calls=None` sets no per-task cap: only the run budget (the
    RunGuard behind the tools) can stop tool calls (the single-agent baseline)."""
    run = AgentRun()
    transcript = prompt
    failed = 0
    while True:
        try:
            response = llm.complete(system=system, prompt=transcript, model=model)
        except RunStopped:

        except LLMFatalError:

        except LLMError as exc:
            response = exc
        run.llm_calls += 1

        f"Critic loops used so far: {board.critic_loops} of {limits.max_critic_loops}."
    )
    return ask(
        llm,
        agent="critic",
        task_id=critic_task_id(loop),
        model=model,
        system=CRITIC_SYSTEM,
        prompt=prompt,
        parse=parse,
        retry_hint="Your last reply was not a valid review",
        max_failed_attempts=limits.max_failed_attempts,
    )

    def evidence_rules(state: Blackboard) -> dict[str, Any]:
        # Only final_confidence goes back on the board (for the Critic). The rules always
        # recompute from the agents' raw entries, so they stay idempotent across loops.
        final = {h.id: h.final_confidence for h in apply_evidence_rules(state).hypotheses}
        return {"hypotheses": [h.model_copy(update={"final_confidence": final[h.id]}) for h in state.hypotheses]}

    def _after_evidence_rules(state: Blackboard) -> Literal["critic", "reporting"]:
        if run_guard.poll() is not None or not state.hypotheses:
            return "reporting"  # a run limit was hit, or there is nothing to critique
        return "critic"

    def critic(state: Blackboard) -> dict[str, Any]:
        answer = review(client("critic"), board=state, model=config.model_for("critic"), limits=limits)
        update: dict[str, Any] = {"counters": answer.counters}
        if answer.failure is not None:
            update["failures"] = [answer.failure]
        verdict = answer.value
        if verdict is None:  # the Critic failed or a run limit stopped it: report as is
            return update
        update["critic_reviews"] = [verdict]
        if not verdict.follow_ups:
            return update  # satisfied
        if state.critic_loops >= limits.max_critic_loops:
            update["terminated_by"] = "critic_limit"
            return update
        update["tasks"] = follow_up_tasks(verdict)
        update["critic_loops"] = state.critic_loops + 1
        return update

    def _after_critic(state: Blackboard) -> Literal["schedule", "reporting"]:
        more = any(t.status == "pending" for t in state.tasks)
        return "schedule" if more and run_guard.stop is None else "reporting"

INCIDENT = "inc01"
SCENARIO = load_scenario(INCIDENT)
SERVICE = SCENARIO.report.affected_service
WINDOW = {
    "start": SCENARIO.report.window_start.isoformat(),
    "end": SCENARIO.report.window_end.isoformat(),
}
_ID = re.compile(r'"id":\s*"([a-z]+:inc\d{2}:[^"]+)"')
_ANY_ID = re.compile(r'"([a-z]+:inc\d{2}:[^"]+)"')

PLAN = {
    "tasks": [
        {"id": "t1", "agent": "observability", "goal": "logs", "depends_on": []},
        {"id": "t2", "agent": "dependency", "goal": "graph", "depends_on": []},
        {"id": "t3", "agent": "reasoning", "goal": "compare", "depends_on": ["t1", "t2"]},
    ]
}


def tool_calls_then_finish(calls: list[dict[str, Any]], marker: str) -> Reply:
    """Call the tools in order, then finish with one fact citing the first ID seen."""

    def reply(prompt: str) -> Any:
        done = prompt.count("Tool result:")
        if done < len(calls):
            return {"action": "call_tool", **calls[done]}
        ids = _ID.findall(prompt)
        facts = [{"statement": marker, "evidence_ids": ids[:1]}] if ids else []
        return {"action": "finish", "facts": facts, "assumptions": []}

    return reply


def reasoning_reply(prompt: str) -> Any:
    ids = sorted(set(_ANY_ID.findall(prompt)))
    return {
        "action": "finish",
        "hypotheses": [
            {"id": "h1", "service": SERVICE, "category": "bad_deploy", "summary": "deploy",
             "supporting_ids": ids[:1], "contradicting_ids": ids[-1:], "proposed_confidence": 0.8},
            {"id": "h2", "service": SERVICE, "category": "capacity", "summary": "cpu",
             "supporting_ids": ids[-1:], "contradicting_ids": ids[:1], "proposed_confidence": 0.3},
        ],
    }  # fmt: skip


def reporting_reply(prompt: str) -> Any:
    return {"recommended_actions": [{"action": "Roll back", "rationale": "r", "hypothesis_id": "h1"}]}


DEFAULT_SCRIPT: dict[str, Reply] = {
    "planner": lambda _: PLAN,
    "observability": tool_calls_then_finish([{"tool": "logs", "args": {"service": SERVICE, **WINDOW}}], "OBS-FINDING"),
    "dependency": tool_calls_then_finish([{"tool": "dependencies", "args": {"service": SERVICE}}], "DEP-FINDING"),
    "reasoning": reasoning_reply,
    "reporting": reporting_reply,
    "critic": lambda _: {"critique": "The evidence is enough.", "follow_ups": []},
}

### User · 2026-11-12T19:49:48.972Z

**Tool result**
```
- **JSON actions instead of native tool calling.** Every backend speaks the same protocol, including local models with weak native tool calling, so each agent replies with a JSON action, `call_tool` or `finish`, validated by Pydantic. The graph node runs the tool and loops. An invalid reply gets one retry. Two failed attempts record an `AgentFailure`, and the run continues.
- **Critic loop.** After the evidence rules, the Critic reviews the hypotheses. It can ask for up to 3 follow-up tasks for the Observability or Dependency agent, plus a Reasoning task that revises the hypotheses. These tasks go back through `schedule`. At most 2 loops run.
- **Limits and `terminated_by`.** One `RunGuard` per run enforces 30 tool calls, 300,000 tokens (input plus output, cache reads excluded) and a 600 s wall clock, for both systems. Each agent task has a cap of 6 tool calls. When a limit trips, no new work starts, and Reporting writes the report from the current blackboard. `terminated_by` is `tool_budget`, `token_budget` or `timeout` for a tripped limit, `critic_limit` when the Critic still wanted more after 2 loops, and `done` otherwise.
```

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T19:50:12.374Z

All three fixes are in, committed as `970352c`. All 356 tests pass and pyright is clean.

**1. An agent that runs out of tool calls keeps its work.** After every tool result, the agent now sees how many calls it has left ("Tool calls left in this task: N of 6"). At zero it's told to finish. The first tool call over the limit is now just a warning that gives one answer-only turn, not a failure. If the agent keeps calling tools after that, it fails as before. In the inc04 run, t2 would have handed in its deploy and config findings instead of losing all 6 calls.

**2. A fact without evidence no longer sinks the whole answer.** A "fact" that cites no evidence IDs is now kept as an assumption, and its note says why. The agent's other facts and hypotheses are kept. This is what wasted c1.1's 6 calls.

**3. The Critic plans within the remaining budget.** Its prompt now says "Tool calls left in this run: N of 30" and asks only for follow-ups the run can still pay for, most important first. If no tool calls are left, the Critic isn't asked at all, and the report says `terminated_by: tool_budget`. Before, it would still ask for 3 follow-ups that then failed immediately.

The single-agent baseline is unchanged. It has no per-task limit, and its prompt already states the run budget.

**Caveats:**
- An uncited fact becomes an assumption while the agent's answer is read, so it doesn't appear in the report's rule adjustments. The assumption's note records the reason instead.
- None of this has been run live yet. A fresh inc04 run is the real check. Fixes 1 and 2 should free about 12 tool calls, but whether the model spends them on the five-day memory check depends on its own choices.

I updated the README's design notes and added ticket 16.

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T20:19:02.437Z

This run is inc04 again, the first live run with the three fixes. It's better than the last one but still INCONCLUSIVE: h1 reached 0.55, just under the 0.6 threshold. This time the run actually retrieved the decisive evidence, then threw it away before any agent could use it.

**What improved compared with the last inc04 run**

| | Last run (6efefa7f) | This run |
|---|---|---|
| Top hypothesis (image-resizer, `capacity`) | 0.45 | 0.55 |
| Key evidence IDs cited by h1 | 3 of 6 | 5 of 6 |
| Tasks that failed | t2 and c1.1 (12 calls lost) | c1.1 only (5 calls lost) |
| Critic's second-round follow-ups | 3 (none could run) | 1 |

- The deploy and config-change task (t2) finished in 3 calls instead of hitting its limit and failing.
- 4 facts with no evidence IDs were kept as assumptions instead of sinking their tasks.
- Both log timeouts were closed again (`gap_closed`).
- No evidence rule lowered any confidence; the reasoning agent itself proposed 0.55.
- The deploy trap (h2, thumbnail-cache `bad_deploy`) stayed second at 0.35. h1 even cites the deploy as contradicting evidence.

**Why it still came out INCONCLUSIVE.** The proof is memory rising slowly over days (820 MiB on Nov 8, 3420 MiB on Nov 12) while traffic stays flat, before the deploy. Both attempts to check that were lost:

1. **Task c1.1** ("distinguish a slow leak from a step change after the deploy") made 5 tool calls. Then both of its final replies failed to parse with `Invalid JSON: expected value at line 1 column 1`. That error means the reply didn't start with `{`, most likely because the model wrote some text before the JSON. The raw reply isn't saved, so I can't see exactly what it said.
2. **Task c2.1** asked the same question again in the Critic's second round. It retrieved `metric:inc04:mem:0003`: 3420 MiB on Nov 12, already near the 4 GiB limit before the 02:50 deploy. Then the run hit its 30-call budget mid-task. When the tool budget trips, all LLM work stops too. So c2.1 never wrote a finding, and the final reasoning pass (c2.r) never ran. The evidence sits on the blackboard, but no hypothesis cites it.

`metric:inc04:mem:0001` itself (Nov 8, 820 MiB) was never retrieved. Even so, `mem:0003` alone would very likely have pushed h1 over 0.6 and refuted the deploy theory.

**Proposed fixes:**
1. **Read JSON that has text around it.** Take the first JSON object in the reply instead of requiring the reply to start with `{`. When a reply still fails, save its first 200 characters in the failure reason so the cause is visible.
2. **Make the tool budget stop only tool calls.** When the 30th call is used, a task that was mid-investigation gets one answer-only turn, so its evidence becomes a finding. Pending reasoning tasks still run, because they need no tools. No new investigation tasks start. The token and wall-clock limits keep stopping everything at once.

Fix 1 is a robustness fix. Fix 2 changes what the tool limit means: today "when a limit trips, no new work starts" (PRD L26). After the change, the tool budget would cap tool calls but not end the run's thinking, while the 300k-token and 10-minute limits still bound it. The baseline would follow the same rule, so the comparison stays fair: when its budget runs out it would get one answer-only turn too.

Should I do both, or only fix 1?

"""Run limits (L26, Q16, D5): one config object and the guard that enforces it.

Both systems (the multi-agent graph and the single-agent baseline) read the same
`RunLimits`, so they are compared under identical budgets:

    from incident_investigator.limits import DEFAULT_LIMITS, RunGuard

`RunGuard` enforces the per-run limits for one investigation. Every LLM call goes
through `GuardedLLM` (or calls `before_llm`/`after_llm` itself) and every tool call
through `RunGuard.tool_call`. The first limit hit trips the guard; after that any
further LLM or tool call raises `RunStopped`, the agent loop stops cleanly, and the
run goes straight to Reporting with `terminated_by` set to the limit's name.

- Tool budget: the call that would exceed `max_tool_calls_per_run` is refused (not
  made, not counted) and trips `tool_budget`.
- Token budget (D5): input + output tokens, cache reads excluded. The call that
  reaches the budget completes (it is already paid for) and trips `token_budget`;
  no new LLM call starts.
- Wall clock: checked before every LLM call, tool call and scheduling step against
  the injectable `clock`. A call already in flight is bounded by the per-call LLM
  timeout (120 s), not interrupted.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import threading
from collections.abc import Callable
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from typing import Literal

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict

from incident_investigator.llm.base import LLMClient, LLMResponse, Usage

TerminatedBy = Literal["done", "tool_budget", "token_budget", "timeout", "critic_limit"]
Clock = Callable[[], datetime]


def utc_now() -> datetime:
    return datetime.now(UTC)


class RunLimits(BaseModel):
    """Every limit of one investigation. The defaults are the locked values (L26, Q16)."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", frozen=True)

    max_tool_calls_per_task: int = 6
    max_tool_calls_per_run: int = 30
    max_tokens_per_run: int = 300_000
    """Input plus output tokens, excluding cache reads (D5)."""
    wall_clock_s: float = 600.0
    max_critic_loops: int = 2
    max_failed_attempts: int = 2
    """Failed attempts (invalid reply, LLM error or timeout) before an AgentFailure."""


DEFAULT_LIMITS = RunLimits()


class RunStopped(Exception):  # noqa: N818 - a stop signal, not an error
    """A run limit was hit; the caller stops its work and keeps what it has."""

    def __init__(self, reason: TerminatedBy) -> None:
        super().__init__(f"run stopped: {reason}")
        self.reason = reason


class RunGuard:
    """Tracks one run's tool calls, tokens and elapsed time. Thread-safe."""
        return dispatch()

        self.inner = inner
        self.guard = guard

    """Tracks one run's tool calls, tokens and elapsed time. Thread-safe."""

    def __init__(self, limits: RunLimits = DEFAULT_LIMITS, *, clock: Clock = utc_now) -> None:
        self.limits = limits
        self.clock = clock
        self.started_at = clock()
        self.tool_calls = 0
        self.tokens = 0
        self._stop: TerminatedBy | None = None
        self._lock = threading.Lock()

    @property
    def stop(self) -> TerminatedBy | None:
        """The limit that stopped the run, or None while it may continue."""
        return self._stop

    def elapsed_s(self) -> float:
        return round((self.clock() - self.started_at).total_seconds(), 3)

    def _trip(self, reason: TerminatedBy) -> None:
        if self._stop is None:
            self._stop = reason

    def _poll_locked(self) -> TerminatedBy | None:
        if self._stop is None and self.elapsed_s() >= self.limits.wall_clock_s:
            self._trip("timeout")
        return self._stop

    def poll(self) -> TerminatedBy | None:
        """Check the wall clock; return the stop reason, if any. Never raises."""
        with self._lock:
            return self._poll_locked()

    def check(self) -> None:
        """Raise RunStopped if the run must stop."""
        with self._lock:
            if (reason := self._poll_locked()) is not None:
                raise RunStopped(reason)

    def before_llm(self) -> None:
        self.check()

    def after_llm(self, usage: Usage) -> None:
        with self._lock:
            self.tokens += usage.tokens_in + usage.tokens_out
            if self.tokens >= self.limits.max_tokens_per_run:
                self._trip("token_budget")

# --- Hitting a limit ----------------------------------------------------------


def test_the_baseline_stops_at_thirty_tool_calls_with_tool_budget_and_still_reports():
    llm = RoleLLM(delay_s=0.0, baseline=always_calls_logs)

    run = investigate(INCIDENT, llm, mode="single")

    report = run.report
    assert report.terminated_by == "tool_budget"
    assert report.metrics.tool_calls == 30, "the run budget binds, not the per-task cap of 6"
    assert run.blackboard.counters.tool_calls == 30
    assert report.failures == [], "a stopped run is not the agent's fault"
    assert report.outcome == "INCONCLUSIVE"
    assert len(llm.prompts["baseline"]) == 31


def test_the_baseline_stops_at_the_token_budget_with_token_budget_and_still_reports():
    llm = TokenHeavyLLM(tokens_in=99_000, baseline=always_calls_logs)

    run = investigate(INCIDENT, llm, mode="single")

    report = run.report
    assert report.terminated_by == "token_budget"
    assert report.metrics.tokens_in + report.metrics.tokens_out == 300_000
    assert len(llm.prompts["baseline"]) == 3
    assert llm.prompts["reporting"] == [], "no LLM call after the token budget is spent"

    run = investigate(INCIDENT, llm, limits=limits)

def _parse(text: str) -> JudgeVerdict:
    stripped = text.strip()
    fenced = _FENCE.match(stripped)
    if fenced:
        stripped = fenced.group(1).strip()
    return JudgeVerdict.model_validate_json(stripped)

### Assistant · 2026-11-12T20:23:45.392Z

Both fixes are in, committed as `cd5e31c`. All 365 tests pass and pyright is clean.

**1. Replies with text around the JSON are accepted.** Every place that reads a model reply now takes the first JSON object in it. Text or code fences before or after the JSON are ignored. That covers the investigating agents, the planner, the Critic, the Reporting agent and the judge. If a reply still fails, its first 200 characters appear in the failure reason, so the next failure like c1.1's will show what the model actually wrote. This also replaced three copies of the same parsing code with one shared helper.

**2. The tool budget now stops only tool calls.**
- When the run's 30th call is used, an agent whose next call is refused gets one answer-only turn. Its evidence becomes a finding.
- Investigation tasks that haven't started are skipped, because they could only finish with nothing.
- Reasoning tasks still run, since they need no tools. The Critic is skipped, and the report says `terminated_by: tool_budget`.
- An agent that keeps calling tools after the warning fails, as before.
- The token budget and the 10-minute wall clock still stop everything at once.
- The single-agent baseline follows the same rule, and its prompt now says it must finish once the budget is spent.

In the last inc04 run, c2.1 would have reported the Nov 12 memory reading of 3420 MiB, from before the deploy. The final Reasoning pass (c2.r) would then have run with that evidence.

Three existing tests changed meaning:
- An agent that ignores the "finish now" turn now counts as an agent failure instead of a clean stop.
- The baseline in that situation now makes 33 LLM calls instead of 31.
- After the budget is spent, Reasoning is still dispatched.

I updated the README, the PRD's limit-hit line and the baseline's description, and added ticket 17. A third live inc04 run is the real check.

### Assistant · 2026-11-13T06:28:31.735Z

This run gets inc04 right: ROOT_CAUSE on image-resizer `capacity` at 0.72, above the 0.6 threshold. The evaluation would score it correct. It's also the first live run where every fix from today mattered.

**The result**
- **h1 at 0.72:** a slow memory leak in image-resizer, with out-of-memory kills at the 4096 MiB limit as the mechanism. It cites the full memory climb: 820 MiB on Nov 8, then 2150, 3420 and 3890 MiB, while traffic stayed flat at 212 to 219 requests per second.
- **5 of 6 key evidence IDs cited,** including the decisive `metric:inc04:mem:0001` that the two earlier runs never found. Only `log:inc04:0004` was never retrieved.
- **The deploy trap is explicitly refuted:** h2 (thumbnail-cache `bad_deploy`) dropped to 0.15. The memory series is listed as evidence against it, and its summary says the multi-day data "largely refutes this as a root cause".
- **No agent failures.** All 11 tasks finished. No rule changed any confidence apart from closing the two log timeouts.
- **Cost:** 30 tool calls, 45 LLM calls, 122k input and 39k output tokens, about 3.6 minutes.

**Which fix did what**
- **Uncited facts:** 3 facts with no evidence IDs were kept as assumptions instead of rejecting their tasks' answers.
- **Gap closure:** both log timeouts were closed, so neither cost any confidence.
- **Budget-aware Critic:** its second review asked for only 1 follow-up, c2.1, which checked whether memory grew over days or jumped after the deploy.
- **Soft tool budget (the decisive one):**
  - The Critic's second review began "h2 sits at the top": after the first follow-up round, the agents had ranked the deploy trap first.
  - c2.1 made 2 tool calls, the run hit 30, and its next call was refused. Under the old rule it would have stopped with nothing.
  - Now it got its answer-only turn and reported the multi-day memory climb. The final Reasoning pass (c2.r) then ran and flipped the ranking to the correct answer.
  - Without this fix, the run would most likely have ended with the deploy trap on top: wrong, and fallen for the trap.

**Across the three inc04 runs**

| Run | Top hypothesis | Confidence | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6efefa7f | image-resizer `capacity` | 0.45 | INCONCLUSIVE |
| cfa12a2e | image-resizer `capacity` | 0.55 | INCONCLUSIVE |
| 52054ab4 | image-resizer `capacity` | 0.72 | ROOT_CAUSE |

**Things to keep in mind**
- This is one run per incident, and the model doesn't answer the same way every time, so this shows the fixes work, not that inc04 will always pass.
- The run used the whole tool budget again. That's fine now that the budget only stops tool calls, but the margin is thin.

Next, I'd re-run inc01 to confirm the gap fix live, then the full evaluation with `uv run eval --all`. That's 20 investigations plus 20 judge calls, billed to your API key.

189-### What the summary measures
190-
191-- **Accuracy** (deterministic, from the answer keys): for a root-cause incident, the outcome is `ROOT_CAUSE` and the top-ranked hypothesis matches the answer key's service and category. For the inconclusive-by-design incident (inc09), the outcome is `INCONCLUSIVE`. There is no partial credit. Each wrong answer gets a reason: `outcome_mismatch`, `no_hypothesis`, `wrong_service`, `wrong_category` or `wrong_service_and_category`.
192-- **Grounding:** the share of cited evidence IDs that exist in the scenario. The citations are the facts' evidence IDs and the hypotheses' supporting and contradicting IDs, **plus the IDs the evidence rules removed from the report** (demoted facts and dropped hypothesis IDs). Without those, every report would score 100%, because the rules remove unknown IDs before the report is built. A report with no citations shows `n/a`.
193-- **Cost, per system:** tool calls, LLM calls, tokens in, tokens out, cache tokens and latency, as means per incident and as totals. Tokens in = uncached input + cache writes. Cache reads are shown on their own and do not count toward the token budget. Compare tokens and latency next to LLM calls: the multi-agent system makes more calls, and each call resends its system prompt.

- **Every report ranked the right answer first.** All 20 top hypotheses named the correct service and category. Both systems correctly answered INCONCLUSIVE on inc09, and both found inc04's memory leak behind the deploy trap.

# Authoring defaults

Read once before writing a fresh candidate. An existing frozen candidate going straight to `finalize` needs this only if repair changes its authorship.

## Composition and meaning

For Architecture, default to a system overview led by the main user journey unless the user asks for a narrower mechanism, module map, or deployment topology. Group cooperating roles in accurately named subsystems when that still explains the requested interaction. Separate roles when grouping would hide control ownership, a trust or persistence boundary, lifecycle behavior, or another distinction the reader asked about. Keep secondary and opt-in capabilities in concise sourced notes unless their path matters to the requested question. Name a deliberately narrower scope in the title.

Preserve every requested responsibility, relationship direction, protocol, and behavior-changing condition. Show approval, authorization, and state-transition gates on the affected node or relationship; a card alone cannot qualify an otherwise unconditional arrow. Keep source evidence with each asserted claim. Boundaries express real isolation, ownership, runtime, or persistence facts. Cards answer additional reader questions; they do not replace required topology. There is no node, edge, source, card, or boundary quota. If an authored overview needs regrouping after failure, use [Composition repair](authoring-contract.md#composition-repair); user-supplied or agreed topology remains fixed.

Relationship labels carry meaning. Give them clear space and preserve action, protocol, direction, async behavior, or cross-boundary meaning. Where the mode schema permits an absent label, omit it only when both endpoints already fully imply it; a collision calls for spacing or routing repair. See [Label repair](authoring-contract.md#label-repair) when measured evidence reports a collision.

## Layout and routing

Place Architecture nodes by their actual connections before assigning coordinates; the router cannot rearrange boxes, so placement decides whether lines stay straight. Classify each relationship first, then place:

- **Main path**: the reader's main journey, neighbors adjacent in reading order. Let a medium path step through meaningful rows instead of making a shallow horizontal strip.
- **Branch or store**: directly above or below the node that owns, reads, or writes it, centered on that node so the edge is one straight segment. Keep all stores and branches of one row on the same side of it.
- **Return** (back to an earlier main-path node): put its source on the side of the main path with no branches or stores, so it runs through an empty corridor instead of crossing them.
- **Second entrance** into a node that already has an incoming edge: place the new source so it reaches that node from another side, usually directly below or above it.
- **Fan-out**: a side with k relationships needs at least `32 + 14 × (k − 1)`px (four need 74px). Spread a hub's counterparts over tw
… [truncated 21110 chars]

### User · 2026-11-13T07:31:04.313Z

**Tool result**
```
### Dataflow

Stages express transformation or custody. Rows separate parallel streams. Give every flow a concise data-contract or movement label; choose its visual convention using the required [Dataflow authoring defaults](authoring-defaults.md#presentation-and-modes).

First-draft shape: declare `stages[]` (each a `{label}`), then place every node with integer `stage` and `row` — not `pos`/`size`. Flows are `flows[]` with required `from`, `to`, and non-empty `label`.

Omit `meta.viewBox` to fit canvas width to all stages and nodes in either quality
profile, including explicit node widths. Width uses content + 24px right padding
with a 480px minimum (five default stages still need ~1068); left-edge overflow
still needs a node repair. An authored viewBox stays fixed. Showcase node growth, readable typography, content height, port bridges
and bounded label placement apply only when the canvas and all node widths are
omitted. `--quality` overrides `meta.quality_profile`; without it, the renderer
uses `ARCHIFY_QUALITY_PROFILE` when set, otherwise the JSON profile.

### ERD
## Repository evidence

When the diagram must reflect real code, inspect repository entrypoints,
runtime boundaries, storage, transports, and deployment configuration before
authoring. Record only evidence you actually verified. `--repo-root <path>` is
accepted by `render`, `validate`, `deliver`, and `preview` for every diagram
type, by architecture `compare`, and by workflow `migrate`; every mode verifies `meta.repository` and
node `sources` the same way. Migrating a source-backed workflow requires the same
`--repo-root` so its candidate is verified before replacing the destination.
Never infer runtime causality from file proximity
or naming alone.

Declare `meta.repository.url` and one full 40-character `revision`, then attach
`sources` to the mode's node collection (Architecture `components[]`, Workflow
and Data Flow `nodes[]`, Sequence `participants[]`, Lifecycle `states[]`) with
repository-relative `path`, optional `line`, `end_line`, and `label`.
Verification reads blobs at that commit, independently of working-tree edits.
Verification ignores local Git replacement refs, including those selected by
`GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE`, and always reads the original objects at the pinned SHA.
It does not change repository configuration or delete replacement refs.
A matching local origin, available commit, bounded path,
blob, and valid line range are required in every link mode. Verification is
local and makes no remote requests; it establishes neither public availability

### User · 2026-11-13T07:32:03.975Z

**Tool result**
```
## Judge scores

An LLM judge (the `judge` role in models.toml, its own prompt) read each report with the answer key and every cited evidence row. It scores evidence support, fact/assumption separation and recommended actions from 1 to 5 (means over the pairs it judged), and says whether the system fell for a planted trap (count of yes over judged pairs). **The judge never decides accuracy**: accuracy above is deterministic, so a judge mistake cannot move it. A judge failure is a pair whose judge output stayed invalid after one retry; it is counted, not scored.

| System | Judged | Evidence support | Fact/assumption separation | Actions | Fell for trap | Judge failures |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| multi | 10 of 10 | 3.90 | 3.10 | 4.60 | 1/10 | 0 |
| single | 10 of 10 | 4.10 | 4.30 | 4.20 | 1/10 | 0 |

### Judge cost (not charged to either system)

The judge's own LLM calls, tokens and latency (D6). They are not in any of the system metrics above.

| Results judged for | LLM calls | Tokens in | Tokens out | Cache tokens | Latency s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| multi | 10 | 153,823 | 12,364 | 0 | 125.8 |
| single | 10 | 54,360 | 9,931 | 0 | 105.9 |

- **Bias (R5):** the judge is a Claude model, like the systems it grades, and may favour their style. Read its scores as a second opinion on reasoning quality, not as ground truth.

## How to read this
 M docs/transcript/5ef28d2e-b5be-498e-a014-0f3f10f82352.jsonl
 M docs/transcript/5ef28d2e-b5be-498e-a014-0f3f10f82352.md
 M eval/results/live/inc01__multi.json
 M eval/results/live/inc01__single.json
 M eval/results/live/inc02__multi.json
 M eval/results/live/inc02__single.json
 M eval/results/live/inc03__multi.json
 M eval/results/live/inc03__single.json
 M eval/results/live/inc04__multi.json
 M eval/results/live/inc04__single.json
2a6b15a Add the live evaluation results (20 of 20 pairs; judge pending)

## Optional capture evidence

`visual-check` remains backward compatible for a requested or escalated
perceptual review:

```bash
node bin/archify.mjs visual-check <output.html> --summary --require-provenance
```

`--summary` returns compact JSON with all diagnostics and absolute paths to the complete receipt, contact sheet, and every screenshot. For a chosen visual review, inspect the relevant captures; capture success is not perceptual approval. `--json` retains the full receipt output for existing consumers. Both modes run the same checks and keep the same exit status. If cleanup fails after publication, the summary retains the final failure diagnostics and `publication` recovery details; the linked receipt records the earlier committed evidence.

It performs the same automated browser measurements, captures light/dark
screenshots at 1440×900 and 2048×1320, and writes four viewport PNG sidecars,
one relative-path HTML contact sheet, and one JSON receipt. `--out-dir <dir>` moves all of these sidecars together.
Open the HTML contact sheet in a browser or inspect the viewport PNGs with an image reader. Its receipt reports `visualReview: "pending"` because captures do not themselves
make a perceptual judgment. Capture and provenance failures follow the ownership rules below.

The receipt, contact sheet, and four PNGs form one owned evidence set. Before
capture, `visual-check` freezes every requested directory entry, its
canonical write slot and physical parent, and the target's absent/file state,
type, device/inode identity, and mode. Hard-linked evidence targets are not safe
write targets. All candidate files are created exclusively inside one random,
private staging directory beneath the physical evidence directory; the receipt
is published last. Each staged candidate must have exactly one hard-link name
before publication. The no-clobber publish link temporarily gives the staged
and final names a link count of two; unlinking the verified staged name must
leave the final entry with a link count of one. An unexpected external hard
link fails closed and its alias is never removed.

Chrome inspects one identity- and content-checked copy of the captured artifact
in a private local temporary directory, so browser file loading does not depend
on UNC or long-path support. The six publication candidates remain on the
evidence volume. Both temporary directories are cleaned without recursively
deleting unknown contents; retained entries include their recovery locations.

Immediately before committing anything, `visual-check` re-resolves and verifies
the complete six-path set. An absent-path claimant, existing-path replacement,
symbolic-link or dangling-link retarget, parent-topology change, hard link, or
indeterminate identity fails closed with `viewer/evidence-path-conflict`. The
claimant and every other final evidence path remain untouched. Cleanup removes

# Incident Investigator

A multi-agent system that investigates simulated production incidents. It finds probable root causes and recommends corrective actions, and every claim cites evidence. When the evidence is too weak, it says `INCONCLUSIVE`.

Six agents work together through a shared, typed blackboard and a task DAG. An Orchestrator plans the work. Observability and Dependency agents gather evidence in parallel through five tools. A Reasoning agent builds competing hypotheses. A Critic can send the investigation back for more evidence. A Reporting agent writes the recommended actions. Deterministic code checks the evidence and sets the final confidence.

The repository also has a single-agent baseline with the same tools, limits and report schema, 10 hand-written incidents with answer keys, an evaluation runner with an LLM judge, a CLI and an HTTP API.

It was built for the AI Engineer assignment in `orange_invest_assignment.md`. The design record is `DECISIONS.md`. The requirements are in `incident-investigator-ai-ready-prd.md`.

- [Setup](#setup)
- [Run an investigation (CLI)](#run-an-investigation-cli)
- [HTTP API](#http-api)
- [Tests](#tests)
- [Evaluation](#evaluation)

- [How the agents coordinate](#how-the-agents-coordinate)

## Architecture

The LLM judge has not run yet**: run `uv run eval --judge-only --run live` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set to add its scores. Raw results and traces are in [`eval/results/live/`](eval/results/live/). The investigations were sent through the HTTP API (`uv run api`), which calls the same `investigate()` as `uv run eval`, and each pair was scored and saved in the runner's own result format, so the runner resumes and judges them as usual.
- **Cost:** multi-agent used about 2.9 times the tool calls, 3.5 times the LLM calls, 4.5 times the tokens and 3.5 times the wall time of the baseline per incident. On these 10 incidents it did not buy higher accuracy; whether it buys better-supported reasoning is what the judge scores are for.
- **Judge scores are pending.** The live investigations ran, but the judge needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the shell: `uv run eval --judge-only --run live`.

<!-- DRAFT by the coding assistant: the project owner edits and signs off this note before submission. -->

# Technical note

## Key decisions

- **LangGraph state as a blackboard.** Six agents never message each other. They write typed Pydantic entries (findings, hypotheses, gaps, failures) to shared state, and reducers merge parallel writes.
- **Hybrid Orchestrator.** One Opus call plans a task DAG; plain Python validates it and runs it in parallel waves. Scheduling, retries and limits stay deterministic.
- **Claude through the API key only.** Every call is one Messages API request. An earlier subscription-CLI backend was removed as too fragile for an unattended service (ADR 0002). Agents reply with Pydantic-validated JSON actions, so local models speak the same protocol.
- **Code, not the LLM, owns confidence.** Facts must cite evidence IDs a tool actually returned. Unsupported hypotheses are capped at 0.5, each evidence gap subtracts 0.1, and below 0.6 the outcome is `INCONCLUSIVE`.
- **Reproducible failures.** Scenarios inject timeouts, partial results and errors on the Nth call to a tool. A turnstile fixes the tool-call order across parallel agents, so the same call fails on every run.
- **A fair baseline.** One Opus agent with the same tools, failure schedule, limits, evidence rules and report schema.
- **Deterministic accuracy.** Accuracy and grounding come from answer keys. An Opus judge scores reasoning quality but never decides accuracy; its cost is reported separately.

## Trade-offs

- The multi-agent system makes more LLM calls than the baseline, and each call resends its system prompt.
- Without native tool calling, the baseline re-sends its transcript each step, so its tokens grow quickly with tool calls.
- Hand-written incidents give reliable answer keys, but only 10, written alongside the system they test.

## Limitations

- **Token accounting.** Cache writes count as input tokens; cache reads are reported separately and excluded from the budget.
- **One run per incident,** without significance tests.
- **The judge is a Claude model,** like the systems it grades, and may favour their style. It also sees the answer key.
- The live evaluation was still pending when this was drafted.
- Only the Anthropic API backend detects rate limits; a local server's 429 is an ordinary failure.
- A gap closes when the same tool later succeeds for the same service, even over a different window.
- inc07 and inc10 have contestable answer keys, with no credit for a neighbouring service.
- The API cannot cancel a run or stream its trace.

## Possible improvements

- Repeat each incident and report variance.
- Use native tool calling and prompt caching to cut tokens per call.
- Close gaps per query window; give partial credit for adjacent services.
- Add a judge from another model family, calibrated against human ratings.
- Add incidents written by someone other than the builder.
- Add API cancellation and live traces.

```
## Coding-assistant transcript

`docs/transcript/` holds the Claude Code sessions that built this project. For each session there are two files:

- `<session-id>.jsonl`: a raw copy of the Claude Code session log.
- `<session-id>.md`: a readable version with user and assistant messages, tool calls and tool results. Long tool outputs are cut at 3,000 characters, and thinking blocks are left out.

A Stop hook writes them: `.claude/hooks/export_transcript.py`, registered in `.claude/settings.json`. It runs after every assistant turn and copies the current session's log, so the transcript is never lost to context compaction. The LLM calls the system itself makes do not trigger it, because they go straight to the Anthropic API and never start Claude Code.

The project owner reviews the transcript for private content before submitting.

## Unfinished features and external dependencies
{
  "hooks": {
    "Stop": [
      {
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "python3 \"$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/hooks/export_transcript.py\" 2>/dev/null || true",
            "timeout": 30
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Stop hook: export the current Claude Code session transcript into docs/transcript/.

Writes <session_id>.jsonl (raw copy) and <session_id>.md (readable) on every turn,
so the full history survives context compaction.
"""

import json
import os
import shutil
import sys
from pathlib import Path

MAX_TOOL_CHARS = 3000


def clip(text: str) -> str:
    return text if len(text) <= MAX_TOOL_CHARS else text[:MAX_TOOL_CHARS] + f"\n… [truncated {len(text) - MAX_TOOL_CHARS} chars]"


def render_block(block) -> str:
    if isinstance(block, str):
        return block
    kind = block.get("type")
    if kind == "text":
        return block.get("text", "")
    if kind == "tool_use":
        args = json.dumps(block.get("input", {}), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
        return f"**Tool call — `{block.get('name')}`**\n```json\n{clip(args)}\n```"
    if kind == "tool_result":
```

{
  "file_path": "/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-<user>-Documents-Learning-Orange/5ef28d2e-b5be-498e-a014-0f3f10f82352/scratchpad/build_submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Build a fresh, redacted submission copy of the repo.\n\nCopies every tracked file (working-tree version, so the newest transcripts) into DEST,\nredacts personal details in docs/transcript/, then scans every copied file and fails\nif any personal detail is left. It never touches the source repo.\n\nUsage: python3 build_submission.py <source repo> <dest dir>\n\"\"\"\n\nimport re\nimport shutil\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n# Order matters: the email before the user name it contains, the path before the bare user name.\nREPLACEMENTS = [\n    (re.compile(r\"<user>@<company>\\.ai\", re.IGNORECASE), \"<work-email>\"),\n    (re.compile(r\"/Users/<user>\"), \"/Users/<user>\"),\n    (re.compile(r\"-Users-<user>\"), \"-Users-<user>\"),\n    (re.compile(r\"<user>\", re.IGNORECASE), \"<user>\"),\n    (re.compile(r\"<company>\", re.IGNORECASE), \"<company>\"),\n    (re.compile(r\"\\bRizul\\b\"), \"<colleague>\"),\n]\nFORBIDDEN = re.compile(r\"<user>|<company>|\\bRizul\\b|/Users/(?!<user>)[A-Za-z]\", re.IGNORECASE)\n\n\ndef main(src: Path, dest: Path) -> int:\n    if dest.exists():\n        print(f\"refusing to overwrite existing {dest}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        return 1\n    files = subprocess.run([\"git\", \"-C\", str(src), \"ls-files\", \"-z\"], capture_output=True, check=True).stdout\n    paths = [p for p in files.decode().split(\"\\0\") if p]\n    for rel in paths:\n        target = dest / rel\n        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        shutil.copy2(src / rel, target)\n\n    for path in sorted((dest / \"docs\" / \"transcript\").iterdir()):\n        text = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        counts = []\n        for pattern, placeholder in REPLACEMENTS:\n            text, n = pattern.subn(placeholder, text)\n            counts.append(n)\n        path.write_text(text, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        print(f\"redacted {path.name}: {counts}\")\n\n    leaks = []\n    for rel in paths:\n        try:\n            text = (dest / rel).read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        except UnicodeDecodeError:\n            continue\n        for m in FORBIDDEN.finditer(text):\n            leaks.append(f\"{rel}: {text[max(0, m.start() - 40):m.end() + 20]!r}\")\n    print(f\"{len(paths)} files copied; {len(leaks)} leaks\")\n    for leak in leaks[:20]:\n        print(\"LEAK\", leak)\n    return 1 if leaks else 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main(Path(sys.argv[1]), Path(sys.argv[2])))\n"
}
